# RFC 0003: RGL 2.4 and 3.0 - One Engine, Golden Masters, and Typed Extension Points

- **Status**: Draft, revision 5
- **Author**: @STRML
- **Created**: 2026-10-31
- **Ships as**: one minor (2.4.0) and one major (3.0.0). "v2.1" was the historical working name; 2.1.0 already exists (CHANGELOG.md:98) and this checkout is 2.3.0 (package.json:3). **2.4.0** carries Phases 1-4, actionable deprecations and non-breaking hygiene, with no engine, as `2.4.0-rc.N` on `next` for at least one week first. **3.0.0** carries Phases 5-7 and the justified breaking changes in §12, as `3.0.0-rc.N` on `next` for at least two weeks first (§15).
- **Repos**: react-grid-layout (RGL), react-draggable (RD), react-resizable (RR)

## Revision history

**Revision 5 (2026-11-01).** Sam, verbatim: "I want more breaking changes if needed". This revision supersedes the earlier compatibility rulings below. Changes, one line per ruling group:

- Release scope: 2.4.0 stays a non-breaking minor with Phases 1-4, the actionable deprecations and hygiene; the engine release becomes 3.0.0, with breaks only where §12 buys clarity, testability or third-party pluggability.
- Inventory: adopt 14 BREAK groups and KEEP groups K1-K7 (§12); positional callbacks stay as the closest call, because argument 7 covers #345 without replacing every consumer's handler.
- React and legacy: 3.0 requires React `>= 18` with no shim, while 2.4.x keeps `>= 16.8`; `/legacy` stays in-package, frozen at v1 behavior, and absorbs the listed changes where possible. The peer floor applies to the whole package.
- Sequencing: warn in 2.4 only when its replacement is already available there; 3.0-only replacements get a JSDoc notice pointing to the 3.0 guide and migration through the codemod, with no premature runtime warning.
- Support and migration: maintain `release/2.4` on the `2.x` dist-tag for a proposed 12 months of security and critical fixes after 3.0.0 ships; add removals, jscodeshift codemod, legacy absorption and migration-guide PRs, plus `consumer-3.0` fixtures in RGL-46.
- Wrapper default: adopt the RGL-owned wrapper div in 3.0 as BREAK 12, the lowest-confidence change, and retain a panel question in §13.

**Revision 4 (round 3 panel, 2026-10-31).** Historical rulings, superseded where revision 5 says so. Three reviewers approved and six asked for revisions; every finding was local to one PR, and the architecture is unchanged. Changes, one line per ruling group:

- Controlled layouts mid-interaction: the latest controlled layout wins (Sam, 2026-10-31, measured master behavior). A measurement on master dragged `a` from cell 0 to 1 to 2, had the parent render the stale cell-1 `onDrag` layout, then released: `onDragStop` reported x=2 while the DOM ended at x=1. Snap-back is today's behavior, and three review rounds of echo inference (content history, fingerprints, an identity brand) each misread a case, so the engine does not classify echoes. The newest controlled layout is pending and applies at release with `RGL_W003`; `data` is patched at once so `renderItem` sees it mid-drag; the one `layoutChange` reports the final layout (§3, §10).
- Engine state and actions: the placeholder is set at every start, `lastProposal` changes only on an accepted step, a start-phase edit is ignored with `RGL_W012`, drops are exempt from the child-id cancel, queue rule 2 re-runs only with a proposal, a rejected nudge emits nothing, the effects-re-run cancel has its own effect, `RGL_W003` has one rule, and `hasOwnProperty` replaces `Object.hasOwn` (§3, §4, §10).
- Phase plan: RGL-42 is folded into RGL-23, RGL-24 and RGL-25, and the reproduce-then-replace requirement is gone; the then-current ruling kept Compactor.type closed (reversed by revision 5); the cancel selector and the nudge step stay local; the barrel exports, the responsive gates and callback order, the then-current hook collision override (replaced by revision 5), one permission function and the wrap mapping are specified (§3, §4, §9, §12).
- Intake and work bounds: every responsive intake path is validated, `maxW`/`maxH` above the grid pass unchanged, `y: Infinity` resolves against a finite bottom, overlapping statics take one sorted sweep, and the exported compaction helpers and the fast horizontal tide are bounded too. §14 now states the bounds, the trust boundary, the warning and the golden diffs exactly as #2328 ships them (§10, §12, §14).
- Bookkeeping: RGL-02, RGL-10, RGL-11a and #2325 merged, RGL-48 and RGL-49 are in review as #2328, the totals are recounted, the SSR store assertion moved to RGL-24, the overlap and Files gaps are closed, and three docs items are added (§9, §11).
- Reviewer acceptance items: the round-3 operator and simplifier findings are acceptance text on their PR rows. Proposed PR splits became commits inside the existing rows (§8, §9, §15).

**Revision 3 (round 2 panel, 2026-10-31).** Nine reviewers, all REVISE, architecture kept. Changes:

- Every interaction has a reducer-assigned `interactionId`. `propose`, `stop` and `cancel` carry it; a mismatch is a no-op with `RGL_W011` (§3).
- A keyboard nudge is one atomic `nudge` action, a no-op while another interaction is active. `dispatchBatch` is gone (§3, §4).
- `stop` runs the full pipeline once more on the last proposal and commits that result, stated the same way in §3 and §4. `ActiveInteraction` gained `lastProposal`.
- Drop: `start{kind:"drop"}` carries the entry cell; the placeholder never reaches a committed or notified layout; a cancelled drop fires no `onDrop` (§3).
- `configAtStart` is gone; `state.config` is frozen while an interaction is active. The container height rides on `start` (§3, T9).
- Two comparisons: `sameGeometry` for notification (SameValueZero, measured against `fast-equals` 4.0.3) and `sameInput` for sync and echoes. A data-only prop change is ingested without `onLayoutChange` (§3).
- Removals mid-interaction apply to the cancel baseline too. Only the latest controlled layout is pending, and lagging echoes lose to the committed result (§3, §10). Revision 4 reverses the echo rule: the latest controlled layout wins.
- `EngineConfig` equality is defined field by field (§3).
- The store has no `dispose()`. It lives in a ref for the component's lifetime; `useSyncExternalStore` owns subscriptions (§3).
- `GridLayout` gained `interactionKey`, the channel `ResponsiveGridLayout` uses to cancel on a breakpoint change (§3).
- A full `useGridLayout` mapping table replaces the "1:1" claim (§3).
- Middleware edits are per kind (drag `x`/`y`, resize `w`/`h`); both result types are bare values (§4).
- `onInteractionCancel` is gone. The then-current cancelled-drop mapping fired onDragStop; revision 5 replaces it with drop-only callbacks (BREAK 2).
- The `renderItem` cache and the `getCompactor` cache are gone (§4).
- `Compactor.type` widening moved to 3.0.0 with `resolveDrag` and `RGL_W004`; the mutating collision helpers stay through 2.4.x, then revision 5 removes them (§4, §6, §12).
- Input bounds and work bounds ship in 2.4.0: one `validateLayoutInput` on every intake path, plus op-count budget tests for the wrap compactor, `correctBounds` and the classic compactors (§14; RGL-48, RGL-49).
- Strategy errors reach `console.error` in production too, and sync-phase throws keep the previous state (§3, §4).
- RD-03 shrinks to the event-type fix; RR-03 drops `element` and `positionCompensation` (§8).
- Golden-diff and changelog scripts and CODEOWNERS are gone (§15).
- 2.4.0 gets an rc on `next`, release/2.4 was planned as the hotfix branch (revision 5 makes it the maintained 2.x line), and both releases have written promote and abort criteria (§15).
- Status rows updated for #2321, #2322, #2323, #2324 and the merged upstream PRs. RGL-44 folded into RGL-23; RGL-48 and RGL-49 added; PRs and triage re-counted (§7, §9).

**Revision 2 (round 1 panel, 2026-10-31).** Nine reviewers, all REVISE. Changes:

- Split the release: 2.4.0 = Phases 1-4 (no engine); 3.0.0 = engine and extension points, rc on `next` for two weeks (§9, §15).
- The reducer works in grid units only. Pointer-to-grid conversion, scroll, grab offset and scale stay in the React binding. `PointerSample` left core (§3).
- Config lives in engine state and changes only through `setConfig`. The reducer's third parameter and the `getConfig` thunk are gone. `EngineConfig` is defined (§3).
- New state shape: committed `layout`, `active.workingLayout`, `lastNotifiedLayout` and `pending`. The version counters and `engine/notify.ts` are gone. Moves never commit (§3).
- One action vocabulary: drop is `start{kind:"drop"}` + `propose` + `stop`/`cancel`; a keyboard nudge is a start-propose-stop batch (§3).
- Live pixel position stays local to the active `GridItem`. `useGridStore` takes a selector (§3).
- Start/stop pairing is an invariant. A cancel or stop veto fires the stop callback with the start layout and `detail.cancelled` (§4, §10).
- Middleware is two typed hooks, `onProposal` and `onResolved`, with separate result types. Errors go to `onError`; no async rethrow (§4).
- `resolveDrag` returns `null` to reject and runs for drag, drop and keyboard moves only. `dragAxis` became `classicResolveDrag` (§4).
- Deleted from the plan: `itemComponent`, the adapter interfaces, the `DropSource` registry, `EdgeScrollConfig`, the activation predicate, `useGridKeyboard` as an export, and the engine export from `/core`. `CollisionStrategy` moved to Later (§4).
- Historical compatibility ruling, superseded for 3.0 by revision 5: exported defaults keep their types; `calcStyle` stays assignable from `React.CSSProperties`; CI runs an API report and consumer compile tests (§6, §11).
- One mutation policy: core functions stop mutating inputs, `moveElement` included (§6, §12).
- `fastVerticalCompactor` output order is no longer changed (C-B3e pinned, §7).
- One dependency path: RD `^4.5.0`, RR `^4.0.2`, `getClientPoint` always, no feature detection. Upstream releases go to `next` first (§8).
- Failure matrix gained rows for stop-before-start, StrictMode, item removal order, `onDrag` echo, middleware throws and vetoes, delayed touch activation, and untrusted dimensions (§10).
- Perf arithmetic corrected, and the O(n^2) collision floor added as a target (§5).
- New sections: Security (§14) and Release (§15). PR plan re-counted (§9).

## Evidence and citation conventions

Every claim about code carries a `file:line` citation. Paths are relative to the repo named in the row or section. RGL paths drop the `src/` prefix where the folder is clear (`core/layout.ts:295` means `src/core/layout.ts:295`).

The citations come from six audits run on 2026-10-31 against RGL `866f646f`, RD `40829e3` (4.7.2) and RR `c6fd4ab` (4.0.2). RGL citations have since been re-checked against `1468c0f` (PR #2304 merged), which only moved lines in `GridItem.tsx`. Revisions 2 and 3 re-read every new citation at `1468c0f`.

**Citation base.** RGL citations are at `1468c0f` unless they say otherwise. Since then master merged the core golden corpus (#2320, `5a57314`), the React traces (#2321, `4265be9`) and RGL-09 (#2322, `8d22cf0`). Only #2322 touched `src/`: it changed `core/calculate.ts` and `core/constraints.ts`, added `core/geometry/rect.ts`, deleted `core/compact-compat.ts`, and removed one comment line at `GridLayout.tsx:54`. So on master, `GridLayout.tsx` citations past `:54` are one lower. Citations of the post-#2322 code say `at 8d22cf0`. Since revision 3, master also merged the op-count harness (#2323, `0fb56270`), RGL-10 as #2326 (`a4a30244`, which replaced the closed #2324 with the same two commits), RGL-11a as #2327 (`11390f01`) and a dev-dependency update (#2325, `de461846`). #2326 changed `core/compactors.ts` and added `core/compaction/classic.ts` and `core/geometry/axis.ts`. #2327 changed `core/layout.ts` and added `core/interaction/resolveDrag.ts` and `core/interaction/dragCollisionIndex.ts`. Citations into `core/compactors.ts` and `core/layout.ts` stay at `1468c0f`. The audits are cited by section:

| Short name   | Scope                                                              |
| ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| rgl-core     | `src/core/**`, `src/extras/**`, with esbuild repros and benchmarks |
| rgl-react    | `src/react/**`, `src/legacy/**`, with jsdom probes                 |
| rgl-tests    | test suites, coverage, CI, the Playwright harness                  |
| draggable    | react-draggable at HEAD, with vitest repros                        |
| resizable    | react-resizable at HEAD, with jest probes                          |
| pluggability | demand evidence from issues and PRs, plus prior art                |

Bare `§N` means a section of this RFC. Audit sections always carry the audit name (rgl-core §3.1). `bench.cjs` and `count.cjs` are the rgl-core audit scripts.

Numbers are quoted from those audits, from a named PR's measurements (#2323, #2326, #2327), from a named round-2 review or a round-3 ruling, or from a measurement in this revision, or they are marked **to measure**. Nothing in this RFC is an estimate dressed as a measurement. Derived figures show their arithmetic.

---

## 1. Summary and Motivation

### Summary

Make RGL v2 do what the v2 epic (#346) promised and did not fully deliver. The work has four parts:

1. **Golden masters first.** Pin current behavior, bugs included, in all three repos before changing any code. Tag each known bug `knownBug`, so every later fix shows up as a reviewed golden diff.
2. **One pure engine.** Today there are three interaction state machines: `GridLayout`, `useGridLayout` and `ResponsiveGridLayout`. Replace them with a single pure reducer in `src/core` that both `GridLayout` and `useGridLayout` drive.
3. **Typed extension points.** Add slots and hooks as plain objects or functions on the existing strategy pattern (`Compactor`, `PositionStrategy`, `LayoutConstraint`). Each one is testable in Jest without React. There is no `plugins=[]` registry.
4. **Measured performance and clarity work.** Fix the hot paths the audits measured, with a committed benchmark baseline. Delete dead and duplicated code.

It ships as a minor and a major, each first as a release candidate on `next` (§15). **2.4.0** carries parts 1 and 4, the bug fixes, the input and work bounds (§14), actionable deprecations and non-breaking hygiene, with no engine. **3.0.0** carries parts 2 and 3 and the breaking changes in §12.

2.4.0 preserves the 2.x public API. 3.0.0 may break a contract only where §12 lists the gain, migration, codemod coverage and legacy handling. Churn alone is rejected. The 2.x support line lets consumers adopt the fixes before migrating the engine.

### Motivation

#### The #346 scorecard

The epic (#346, opened 2016-09-12) listed the goals below. v2 (RFC 0001) met some of them. The rest are still open, and the code shows why.

| #346 goal                                                      | v2 status           | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Pluggable compactor                                            | **Met**, with a gap | `Compactor` is public (`core/types.ts:262`). Drag-time collision is still hard-wired to `compactor.type` (`core/layout.ts:296-301`, `:383-384`), and `type` is a closed union (`core/types.ts:264`). A custom compactor cannot change what happens during a drag (rgl-core X1).                 |
| Pluggable DOM layout (`top/left` vs `transform`)               | **Partly met**      | `PositionStrategy` exists (`core/position.ts:267-330`). Its `type` is `"transform" \| "absolute"` (`core/types.ts:318`), so a third-party strategy has to pretend to be a built-in. It covers drag start only, not resize or the pixel-to-grid inverse (rgl-react §5.1).                        |
| CSS transition support (#571)                                  | **Not met**         | CSS classes only. Enter and exit animation is impossible (pluggability §2).                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Per-item pluggability                                          | **Partly met**      | Per-item `constraints` exist. There is no per-item compaction policy, because only `static` is checked (`core/compactors.ts:194,234`).                                                                                                                                                          |
| One overridable `<GridItem>` as the primary extension point    | **Not met**         | `GridItem` is exported, but `GridLayout` hard-codes it (`react/components/GridLayout.tsx:1194`, `:1275`). The custom-child contract (forwardRef, `style`, `className`, handlers, `{children}`) is still the top support thread: #1241 and #299 have 50 comments between them (pluggability §2). |
| One way to define a grid                                       | **Not met**         | `data-grid` still works in v2 `GridLayout` (`GridLayout.tsx:248`), although RFC 0001 §3 and README:66 say it is legacy-only (rgl-react §1.9).                                                                                                                                                   |
| Expose utils                                                   | **Met**             | `react-grid-layout/core` exports the algorithms. Constraints run only in `GridItem`, so non-React callers bypass them (`GridItem.tsx:454-464`; rgl-core X8).                                                                                                                                    |
| Pre- and post-compaction data in callbacks (#345)              | **Not met**         | `onDragStop` passes the post-compaction `layout` with the pre-compaction `newItem`. The pre-compaction layout is never exposed (`GridLayout.tsx:614-617`; pluggability §1).                                                                                                                     |
| Documented grid lifecycle                                      | **Not met**         | No published state machine. rgl-react §1 had to reconstruct it from 30+ refs and state fields (`GridLayout.tsx:394-438`).                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Keyboard moves and reading order (comment by tambaqui)         | **Not met**         | `grep onKeyDown\|aria-` over `src/` finds only `aria-hidden` on GridBackground (pluggability §1).                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| "Some cleaning up and (finally) some unit tests" (STRML, 2016) | **Partly met**      | 609 Jest tests exist (rgl-tests §0), but most interaction assertions are bare `toHaveBeenCalled()`: 40 in `test/spec/lifecycle-test.js` and 10 in `test/spec/backcompat-test.js` (rgl-tests §3.1).                                                                                              |

#### What the audits found

Four problems account for most of the findings.

1. **Three engines that drifted apart.** `GridLayout.tsx` (1346 lines) holds config resolution, layout sync, change notification, the drag, resize and drop controllers, the touch adapter and placeholder rendering (rgl-react §3.1). `useGridLayout` is a separate implementation that `GridLayout` never calls. It loops forever on an inline layout literal (`react/hooks/useGridLayout.ts:207-221`) and fires `onLayoutChange` on every drag move (rgl-react §4.9). Every new hook added today would need two implementations.
2. **Mutation where the docs promise immutability.** `moveElement` mutates the live state item (`core/layout.ts:290-292`). That corrupts `oldLayoutRef` (`GridLayout.tsx:548`) and causes a spurious `onLayoutChange` after a drag that ends where it started (rgl-core B2). The classic compactors mutate their input, and the output depends on that mutation (`core/compactors.ts:64-71`; rgl-core M1). RFC 0001's principle 3, "Immutable by default", does not hold.
3. **Hot paths that scale badly.** One drag frame at 500 mixed-size items takes 48 ms in core alone (rgl-core §3.1), three times the 16 ms frame budget RFC 0001 set (`rfcs/0001:1527`).
4. **Closed extension points.** Closed unions (`core/types.ts:264,318`), a key whitelist that drops custom item fields (`core/layout.ts:81-99`), and render paths with no slots (`GridLayout.tsx:1271`).

The maintainer's brief for this work: extensive golden-master and integration testing in all three repos, then serious work on performance and clarity, with extension points broad enough that third-party libraries can add almost anything.

### Design Principles

RFC 0001's five principles stand. This RFC makes principles 1 and 3 true in the code, and adds five more.

6. **Pin before you change.** No refactor lands without a golden suite that covers the code it touches. A behavior change is a golden diff, reviewed like code.
7. **One engine.** Interaction state lives in one pure reducer, in grid units. Components render that state and translate DOM events into grid-unit actions. Nothing else.
8. **Every extension point is a plain value.** An object or a function with typed inputs and outputs. A Jest test without React or a DOM can exercise it. No registry, no ordering keys, no lifecycle beyond what the type says.
9. **Defaults are the extension points.** Built-in behavior goes through the same interface a third party would use: `verticalCompactor.resolveDrag` is the default drag resolver, not a special case.
10. **Measure, then optimize.** Each performance PR states its baseline and target from the committed benchmark JSON.

## 2. Non-Goals

- **Breaking changes only where listed in §12.** Each must buy clarity, testability or third-party pluggability, carry a migration row, and be absorbed by `/legacy` where possible. 2.4.0 remains non-breaking; the API report and consumer fixtures enforce that boundary (§11).
- **No generic plugin registry.** The pluggability survey found no case that ordered arrays of typed strategies cannot cover (pluggability §3, §5). TipTap-style priority ordering is the cost a registry brings.
- **No virtualization.** Searches for "virtualization OR virtualize" returned 0 issues (pluggability §2).
- **No undo/redo feature, and no built-in adapters for external stores (redux, zustand).** No demand was found (pluggability §2). The `detail.source` tag plus a controlled `layout` is enough to build either outside the library.
- **No CSS Grid or flexbox layout mode.** That would be a new `PositionStrategy`, possible after this RFC, not part of it.
- **No rewrite of react-draggable or react-resizable in this release train.** Their bugs get fixed upstream, and RGL talks to them through internal translation functions (§3, §8).
- **No change to the legacy v1 API surface**, other than fixing places where the legacy wrappers already break v1 behavior (RX-13 in §7).
- **2.4 keeps mutable defaults and item fields; 3.0 freezes defaults and types them readonly.** `defaultConstraints` is mutable (`core/constraints.ts:381`), its sinks take mutable arrays (`:400-401,447-448`), and `LayoutItem` fields are mutable (`core/types.ts:37-107`). Widen the sinks to readonly arrays in 2.4; apply BREAK 5 in 3.0.
- **No engine export from `react-grid-layout/core` in 2.4.0 or 3.0.0.** It stays internal until the store goes public (§3, §4 rank 14).

### Decisions already made (not reopened here)

These were settled before this RFC. They are listed here so reviewers do not reopen them.

1. **Golden masters land first in all three repos**, pinning current behavior including bugs. Each known bug carries a `knownBug` tag in its fixture, so a fix is a deliberate, reviewed golden diff. Merged: the RGL core corpus under `test/golden/` (#2320), the RGL React traces under `test/golden-react/` (#2321), and jsdom traces with browser cross-checks in RD (RD #838) and RR (RR #281).
2. **Community PR #2304 lands before the React-layer traces.** Done: it merged as `1468c0f`. It fixes the live drag and resize cell-snap regression (#2303, RX-1 below) by re-rendering only the active item. It touches `react/components/GridItem.tsx`, `test/e2e/live-interaction.spec.ts` and `test/spec/live-interaction-test.tsx` (`gh pr view 2304`). The React traces then pin the fixed behavior, not the regression.
3. **Extend the strategy objects; do not add a registry.** Every extension point in §4 follows from this.
4. **Fix the double `onLayoutChange` outright in a minor, with a changelog note** (RX-5, C-B2). No opt-in flag. The second call carries the same payload, and a flag would keep the bug alive.
5. **React peer `>= 16.8.0` for 2.4.x, `>= 18` for 3.0.** The declared peer is `>= 16.3.0` (`package.json:184-185`); 2.4 corrects it to the hooks floor (RGL-08). The 3.0 store uses React's native `useSyncExternalStore`, with no shim dependency or tsup entry. Keyboard ids use `useId`. CI tests 18 and 19; only the maintained 2.x line has a best-effort 16.8/17 smoke test (§11).
6. **Ship one minor, 2.4.0, and one major, 3.0.0.** Phases 1-4 ship first, then Phases 5-7 and §12's removals, with separate rc windows (§15).
7. **Round 1 rulings (revision 2).** The release split, the grid-unit reducer, the state shape, start/stop pairing, the middleware shape, `resolveDrag` returning `null`, one mutation policy, one dependency path and the deletions listed in the revision history.
8. **Round 2 rulings (revision 3).** Interaction ids and the atomic nudge, the stop pipeline, the two comparisons, the ref-held store, input and work bounds in 2.4.0, the deletions and the release criteria listed in the revision history.
9. **Op-count budgets gate CI.** The harness from #2323 fails on any counter increase (§5). Milliseconds never gate. This was open question 4 in revision 2.
10. **Middleware runs after constraints**, and an edit re-runs them (§4). This was open question 5 in revision 2.
11. **3.0 compactors have `name: string` and required `resolveDrag`.** Remove `Compactor.type` and the fallback; custom authors supply the drag semantics. `PositionStrategy` also gains `name` and optional `itemClassName`. `CompactType` remains exported for legacy props and sorting (`core/types.ts:211`; `core/sort.ts:25-34`). This reverses decision 11 from revision 4.
12. **Round 3 rulings (revision 4).** The latest controlled layout wins (Sam, 2026-10-31, measured master behavior); the engine keeps no echo history and classifies nothing (§3 controlled-layout contract); fold RGL-42 into RGL-23, RGL-24 and RGL-25 (§9); the deletions and the intake and work-bound rules listed in the revision history.

13. **Revision 5 ruling.** Adopt the 14 BREAK groups, K1-K7, the 2.4 deprecation sequence, in-package v1 legacy absorption, the maintained 2.x line and the migration deliverables (§12, §15). Sam's "if needed" rejects churn. BREAK 12 is adopted with panel review in §13.

## 3. Architecture

The engine, the store and the binding in this section ship in **3.0.0**. 2.4.0 changes no architecture: it adds tests and CI, fixes bugs in place, bounds untrusted input and refactors `src/core` (§9). The module tree marks the release that touches each file.

### Target picture

```
 DOM events          React binding (src/react)
 ----------          ------------------------------------------
 mouse, touch  --->  RD / RR translation, getClientPoint
 keyboard            activation (distance, delay)
 HTML5 drop          px -> grid units (rect, scroll, grab, scale)
 touch drop          live pixel box of the active item (local)
                     ignore-until-release after a rejected start
                          |                        ^
                          | GridAction             | selected state
                          | (grid units)           | + GridEvent[]
                          v                        |
 +---------------------------------------------------------------+
 | core engine (src/core/engine): pure, no React, no DOM types    |
 |                                                               |
 |   reduceGrid(state, action) -> { state, events }              |
 |                                                               |
 |   propose -> constraints -> onProposal -> resolveDrag or      |
 |   resizeElement -> compact -> onResolved -> working layout    |
 |   stop -> the same stages on the last proposal -> commit      |
 |        -> layoutChange (at most once)                         |
 +---------------------------------------------------------------+
```

The engine is a reducer, not a class. Its state is plain data in grid units. The React binding owns DOM measurement, pointer math and event translation. The engine owns every decision about where items go.

### Where the engine lives

The audits proposed two locations: `src/react/state/gridReducer.ts` (rgl-react §3.4) and `src/core/engine.ts` (pluggability §3.0). This RFC picks **`src/core/engine/`**. It must be pure and testable without React (principle 8), and `src/core` is the folder that promises that. The React binding (`createGridStore`, `useGridStore`) lives in `src/react/state/`.

The engine is **not exported** from `react-grid-layout/core` in 3.0. Exporting `reduceGrid` would freeze `GridEngineState` and `GridAction` as public API, which is the same reason the store stays internal (§4, Later). Non-React callers who need constraints get the pure functions that are already exported (`applyPositionConstraints`, `applySizeConstraints`, `core/constraints.ts:400,447` at `2182f06a`).

### Engine config

```ts
// src/core/engine/types.ts
export interface EngineConfig {
  readonly cols: number;
  readonly maxRows: number;
  readonly rowHeight: number;
  readonly margin: readonly [number, number];
  readonly containerPadding: readonly [number, number] | null;
  readonly containerWidth: number; // the `width` prop
  readonly isBounded: boolean;
  readonly isDraggable: boolean;
  readonly isResizable: boolean;
  readonly compactor: Compactor;
  readonly constraints: readonly LayoutConstraint[];
  readonly middleware: readonly InteractionMiddleware[];
}

export function sameConfig(a: EngineConfig, b: EngineConfig): boolean;
```

`EngineConfig` holds the strategies and the geometry that `ConstraintContext` needs (`core/types.ts:361-382`), plus `containerPadding`, which `ConstraintContext` lacks today. It holds no user callbacks and no DOM types. The one per-gesture DOM measurement, the container height for bounded resize, rides on the `start` action instead (T9). The input bounds are constants in `core/validation.ts` (§14), not config.

**Config equality.** `sameConfig` compares field by field:

| Field                                                                                       | Equal when                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `cols`, `maxRows`, `rowHeight`, `containerWidth`, `isBounded`, `isDraggable`, `isResizable` | SameValueZero                                                                                                                       |
| `margin`, `containerPadding`                                                                | both `null`, or the same length and each element SameValueZero                                                                      |
| `compactor`                                                                                 | the same `name`, `allowOverlap`, `preventCollision` (`undefined` reads as `false`), `compact` reference and `resolveDrag` reference |
| `constraints`, `middleware`                                                                 | the same length and each element the same reference                                                                                 |

The binding dispatches `setConfig` only when `sameConfig(next, lastDispatched)` is false. An inline `gridConfig={{margin:[10,10]}}` is therefore not a change, and it never triggers `RGL_W003`. A middleware or constraint object rebuilt on every render (`middleware={[snap(5)]}`) is a new reference, so it is a change. The docs say to create those once, at module scope or in `useMemo`. Mid-drag such a change waits in `pending.config` and applies at the flush without a warning, because it changes none of the fields below.

An idle `setConfig` always stores the new config. It re-syncs and commits only when `cols`, `maxRows` or the compactor key changed. The same three fields are the one rule for `RGL_W003` on a config: a pending `setConfig` applied at the flush warns only when it changed `cols`, `maxRows` or the compactor key. A width change or a rebuilt middleware array mid-drag applies silently. §10 and §15 point here.

### Engine state

```ts
export type InteractionKind = "drag" | "resize" | "drop";
export type InteractionSource = "pointer" | "keyboard";
export type InteractionPhase = "start" | "move" | "stop";
export type Proposal = DragProposal | ResizeProposal;

export interface ActiveInteraction {
  readonly interactionId: number; // assigned by an accepted start
  readonly kind: InteractionKind;
  readonly source: InteractionSource;
  readonly itemId: string;
  readonly handle?: ResizeHandleAxis; // resize only
  readonly containerHeight?: number; // from start, when isBounded
  readonly startItem: LayoutItem; // cloned at start
  readonly startLayout: Layout; // cancel target, minus removed items
  readonly workingLayout: Layout; // last accepted step, not committed
  readonly placeholder: LayoutItem; // set by start (below)
  readonly lastProposal: Proposal | null; // last accepted proposal
  readonly lastEmitted: Layout; // argument 1 of the latest move event
}

export interface PendingSync {
  readonly layout?: Layout; // latest syncProps or setLayout
  readonly itemIds?: readonly string[];
  readonly config?: EngineConfig; // latest setConfig
}

export interface GridEngineState {
  readonly config: EngineConfig; // frozen while active is set
  readonly layout: Layout; // committed, compacted
  readonly active: ActiveInteraction | null;
  readonly lastNotifiedLayout: Layout; // baseline for layoutChange
  readonly pending: PendingSync | null;
  readonly nextInteractionId: number;
}
```

- **One config.** `state.config` cannot change while `active` is set, because `setConfig` goes to `pending.config` then. Every step reads `state.config`. There is no per-interaction copy.
- **Interaction ids.** The reducer assigns `nextInteractionId` on an accepted `start` or `nudge`, then increments it. A rejected start assigns nothing. The counter lives in state, so replayed action lists give the same ids and the replay goldens stay deterministic. The binding reads the id from the `start` event that `dispatch` returns.
- At start, an accepted `start{kind:"drag"|"resize"}` sets `placeholder` to `cloneLayoutItem(startItem)` and leaves `lastProposal` `null`, which still marks a click after activation at stop. `start{kind:"drop"}` sets `lastProposal` to `at` and `placeholder` from its start step, so a drop released in its entry cell runs the stop pipeline. After that, `lastProposal` changes only on an accepted `propose`. A step dropped by a veto, a `null` from `resolveDrag` or a throw leaves it alone, so a release after a rejected move re-runs the last accepted proposal, never the rejected one.
- Today `onDrag` and `onResize` receive the layout before compaction as argument 1 (`onDragProp(newLayout, ...)` at `GridLayout.tsx:584`, `onResizeProp(finalLayout, ...)` at `:747-754`), and §4 keeps that argument. A controlled `onDrag={l => setLayout(l)}` therefore sends back the latest step's resolved layout. `lastEmitted` holds that layout (`startLayout` until the first step). It is one value, not a history: the flush uses it to recognize a pending layout that says nothing newer than the engine's own latest output (flush step 3).
- There is no version counter. `layoutChange` is decided by one comparison at the commit boundary, against `lastNotifiedLayout`.

### Actions

All coordinates are grid units. The binding converts pointer pixels before it dispatches.

```ts
export interface DragProposal {
  readonly x: number;
  readonly y: number;
}
export interface ResizeProposal {
  readonly w: number;
  readonly h: number;
  readonly x?: number; // n / w handles move the origin
  readonly y?: number;
}
export interface DropItemSpec {
  readonly i?: string; // default "__dropping-elem__"
  readonly w: number;
  readonly h: number;
  readonly data?: unknown;
}
export type NudgeDelta =
  | { readonly kind: "move"; readonly dx: number; readonly dy: number }
  | { readonly kind: "resize"; readonly dw: number; readonly dh: number };

export type GridAction =
  | { type: "syncProps"; layout: Layout; itemIds: readonly string[] }
  | { type: "setConfig"; config: EngineConfig }
  | { type: "setLayout"; layout: Layout }
  | {
      type: "start";
      kind: "drag" | "resize";
      itemId: string;
      handle?: ResizeHandleAxis; // resize only
      containerHeight?: number; // measured at start when isBounded
    }
  | {
      type: "start";
      kind: "drop";
      item: DropItemSpec;
      at: DragProposal; // the cell the drag entered
      containerHeight?: number;
    }
  | { type: "propose"; interactionId: number; to: Proposal }
  | {
      type: "stop";
      interactionId: number;
      to?: Proposal; // hook path; GridLayout omits it
      dropId?: string; // drop only: the final item id
    }
  | { type: "cancel"; interactionId: number; reason: CancelReason }
  | { type: "nudge"; itemId: string; delta: NudgeDelta };

export type CancelReason =
  | "escape" // only with the keyboard prop (§4)
  | "pointercancel" // touchcancel, or effects re-run mid-gesture
  | "itemRemoved" // raised by syncProps, never dispatched
  | "breakpointChange" // interactionKey changed (below)
  | "dropLeft"
  | "vetoed" // middleware returned false at stop
  | "error"; // a strategy threw at stop

export function reduceGrid(
  state: GridEngineState,
  action: GridAction
): { state: GridEngineState; events: readonly GridEvent[] };

export function createInitialState(
  config: EngineConfig,
  layout: Layout, // validated and normalized by the binding
  itemIds: readonly string[]
): { state: GridEngineState; events: readonly GridEvent[] };
// Runs sync + compact synchronously. lastNotifiedLayout starts as the
// props layout, so events holds the mount layoutChange exactly when
// 3.0 notifies only for a real geometry/flag change (RX-17).
```

- **Identity.** `propose`, `stop` and `cancel` carry the `interactionId` of the interaction they belong to. A gesture uses the id its `start` returned. A cancel the binding raises without a gesture of its own (`interactionKey`, effects re-running, the drop controller) reads `active.interactionId` from the snapshot. When it differs from `active.interactionId`, or `active` is `null`, the action is a no-op: the same state, plus one `warning` event `RGL_W011`, which the binding shows in development once per grid. A keyboard nudge, a second pointer, a second drop source or a hook action carrying another interaction id therefore cannot drive someone else's interaction (§10).
- **A `start` while `active` is set** is rejected: the same state, no events, no id. The binding sees no `start` event and puts that gesture in the ignore-until-release state, so it never dispatches a `propose` for it.
- **Nudge** is one atomic action. While `active` is set it is a no-op with no events. Otherwise the reducer checks permission with the same function as a pointer start (below), assigns an id and runs start, one step and stop through the full pipeline with `source: "keyboard"`. A resize nudge uses `se` geometry. The result commits at once. A rejected nudge is atomic too: when the permission check, a veto, a `null` from `resolveDrag` or a throw rejects it, the reducer returns the previous state, `nextInteractionId` included, and emits no interaction events at all, so no `onDragStart` fires without its stop. A throw still emits its `error` event. `NudgeDelta` is a discriminated union, so a delta that mixes move and size fields does not type-check.
- **The stop pipeline.** `stop` runs the full pipeline once more on the last proposal (constraints, `onProposal`, `resolveDrag` or `resizeElement`, `compact`, `onResolved`) and commits that result. The proposal is `stop.to` when given, else `active.lastProposal`. A stop with neither (a click after activation) runs nothing and commits `startLayout` unchanged. At stop, `resolveDrag` returning `null` commits the last accepted working layout, middleware returning `false` is a cancel with `vetoed`, and a throw is a cancel with `error`.
- **Same-cell moves.** A `propose` whose `to` equals `lastProposal` returns the same state and emits one `move` event. So `onDrag` still fires on every pointer move, as `GridItem.tsx:565` does today, while the store selector sees no change (T7).
- **Drop.** `start{kind:"drop"}` creates the placeholder `{i: item.i ?? "__dropping-elem__", x: at.x, y: at.y, w, h, data}` in `workingLayout` and runs the start step on it. The placeholder lives only in `workingLayout`. At stop, after the pipeline: with `dropId` (the hook path) the placeholder is renamed to that id, as `useGridLayout.ts:414-425` does today, and the result commits. Without it (the `GridLayout` path) the `onDrop` mapping receives the working layout and the placeholder item, as `commitDrop` passes them today (`GridLayout.tsx:1037-1042`), and the commit is the working layout minus the placeholder, recompacted (`GridLayout.tsx:921-927`). The placeholder id never reaches `state.layout`, `lastNotifiedLayout` or a `layoutChange` payload (#2210, `GridLayout.tsx:511`). A cancelled drop fires no `onDrop`.
- **`syncProps.layout`** comes from the `layout` prop and child ids, then `validateLayoutInput` (§14). The engine never reads `data-grid`. It fills a missing child id with the documented default item. Today's `data-grid` construction (`GridLayout.tsx:247-266`) moves to the legacy adapter, including responsive `reSeedFromDataGrid` (`ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:120-148,373-377`).
- One permission function decides whether an item may start, for pointer and keyboard starts alike. A boolean `item.isDraggable` (drag, move nudge) or `item.isResizable` (resize, size nudge) wins over the grid's `isDraggable` or `isResizable`. Without one, a `static` item is blocked and the grid setting decides. That is today's rule (`GridLayout.tsx:1180-1186`), and the drag override is the one `moveElement` honors (`core/layout.ts:277`). The reducer tests cover both override directions for each kind: an item with `isDraggable: true` in a grid with `isDraggable: false`, and an item with `isDraggable: false` in an enabled grid, plus the same pair for `isResizable`.

### Events

```ts
export interface InteractionDetail {
  readonly interactionId: number;
  readonly kind: InteractionKind;
  readonly source: InteractionSource;
  readonly phase: InteractionPhase;
  readonly itemId: string;
  readonly handle?: ResizeHandleAxis;
  readonly startItem: LayoutItem;
  readonly startLayout: Layout;
  readonly proposed: LayoutItem; // after constraints; the onProposal input
  readonly layoutBefore: Layout; // working layout before this step
  readonly layoutResolved?: Layout; // after resolveDrag/resizeElement
  readonly layoutCompacted?: Layout; // after compact
  readonly placeholder: LayoutItem;
  readonly cancelled?: true; // stop callbacks after cancel or veto
  readonly cancelReason?: CancelReason;
  readonly rejectedBy?: string; // "middleware:<name>", "resolveDrag"
}

export type GridEvent =
  | { type: "interaction"; detail: InteractionDetail }
  | { type: "layoutChange"; layout: Layout } // at most once per commit
  | { type: "warning"; code: WarningCode; message: string }
  | {
      type: "error";
      error: unknown;
      source: string; // e.g. "middleware:snap", "compactor:wrap"
      phase: InteractionPhase | "sync";
    };
```

`proposed` is the proposal after constraints, which is what `onProposal` receives. After an edit, later stages and the stored detail see the edited proposal.

The reducer catches every throw from a strategy and returns it as an `error` event. It never rethrows and never schedules work, so it stays pure. Phase `"sync"` covers `createInitialState`, `syncProps`, `setConfig` and `setLayout`. A throw there keeps the previous state. `createInitialState` has no previous state, so it keeps the validated input layout without compaction. The binding reports every `error` event (§4, rank 4).

### What commits

"Commit" means `state.layout` changes and the notification comparison runs. Moves never commit, so `onLayoutChange` never fires mid-drag. That keeps today's rule at `GridLayout.tsx:501-504`, which suppresses the notification while `activeDrag` or `droppingDOMNode` is set.

| Action               | Commits?                                                                       | Then                                                                                                                                                               |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `start`              | No                                                                             | Sets `active` and its id, emits phase `start`                                                                                                                      |
| `propose`            | No                                                                             | On an accepted step, updates `workingLayout`, `placeholder` and `lastProposal` and emits phase `move` (also for a same-cell move). A rejected step changes nothing |
| `stop`               | Yes: the stop pipeline's result                                                | Emits phase `stop`, flushes `pending` (queue rule 5), then at most one `layoutChange`                                                                              |
| `cancel`             | Yes: reverts to `startLayout`                                                  | Emits phase `stop` with `cancelled`, flushes `pending`, then at most one `layoutChange`                                                                            |
| `nudge`              | Yes                                                                            | Emits phases `start`, `move` and `stop`, then at most one `layoutChange`. A rejected nudge emits no interaction events                                             |
| `setLayout`          | Yes (idle); held in `pending.layout` mid-interaction                           | At most one `layoutChange`                                                                                                                                         |
| `syncProps` (idle)   | Yes                                                                            | At most one `layoutChange`                                                                                                                                         |
| `syncProps` (active) | No; see the queue rules                                                        |                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `setConfig` (idle)   | Stores the config; commits when `cols`, `maxRows` or the compactor key changed | At most one `layoutChange`                                                                                                                                         |
| `setConfig` (active) | No; held in `pending.config`                                                   |                                                                                                                                                                    |

### Queue rules for an active interaction

1. **The active item is removed** (drag and resize only). A `syncProps` whose `itemIds` lack `active.itemId` cancels with `itemRemoved`. The cancel target is `startLayout` minus that item, so the stop callback's layout never contains it. Then the sync applies as an idle sync. A drop is exempt: its placeholder has no child, so `itemIds` never contain it. During a drop, a child removal follows rule 2, every other change follows rule 3, and the drop continues.
2. **Another item is removed.** The removal applies at once to both `workingLayout` and `startLayout`, and the step re-runs on `lastProposal`, so the dragged item can move into the freed space. With `lastProposal === null` nothing re-runs. A re-run emits no `move` event. A later stop or cancel can never bring the item back.
3. **Everything else** in a mid-interaction `syncProps` or `setLayout` does two things. It replaces `pending.layout` and `pending.itemIds`, so the newest controlled layout wins. It also patches each item's `data` reference, by id, onto `state.layout`, `active.startLayout` and `active.workingLayout` at once, so `renderItem` and middleware see the newest `data` mid-drag. `data` is never geometry, so patching it at once is safe. There is no echo classification: the engine does not try to tell a parent's copy of its own output from an instruction.
4. **`setConfig`** replaces `pending.config`.
5. **Flush**, on `stop` and `cancel`, in this order:
   1. Commit the interaction result: the stop pipeline's result, or the cancel target.
   2. Apply `pending.config` as an idle `setConfig`. It emits `RGL_W003` only under the config rule above (cols, `maxRows` or the compactor key changed).
   3. If `pending.layout` is `sameInput`-equal, by id, to `active.lastEmitted`, drop it: the parent has only repeated the engine's latest output, so it carries no instruction, and the stop pipeline's decision (an edit, a veto, a `null` from `resolveDrag`) or the cancel target stands. Otherwise apply `pending.layout` as an idle `syncProps` after the interaction result is committed. If its geometry differs from the committed result (`sameGeometry` by id), emit `RGL_W003` with the item ids. `onDragStop` still reports the gesture's result.
   4. Compare once against `lastNotifiedLayout` with `sameGeometry`, and emit at most one `layoutChange`. It reports the final layout, so callbacks agree with the DOM. Today they do not: in the measured case `onDragStop` says x=2 while the grid shows x=1.

**The controlled-layout contract.** The newest controlled layout wins at release. A parent that renders `onDrag`'s layout on every move gets the gesture's own result, including a stop-phase edit or veto, because its latest layout equals `lastEmitted` and is dropped at the flush. A parent whose latest layout differs from `lastEmitted` (it lags, debounces, or sends any other layout) gets its own layout, with `RGL_W003`. Equality is by content (`sameInput`), so a clone or mapped copy of the latest `onDrag` layout counts as current and is dropped like the original; only a stale copy wins. A debounced update that lands after stop is an idle sync and wins too, as a late prop update does today. The docs (RGL-41) say to persist in `onDragStop` or `onLayoutChange`, and not to feed throttled `onDrag` output back. They also call out the data case: a parent that changes only an item's `data` mid-drag while still holding its pre-drag layout sends that pre-drag geometry too, so the drag reverts at release with `RGL_W003`. To update `data` during a drag, build the new layout from the latest `onDrag` argument, or hold the update until `onDragStop`. RGL-23 pins the cases with reducer tests, including that data case.

**Batching is a test, not an assumption.** RGL-24 pins the measured case: a controlled parent sets its layout from `onDrag`'s argument 1, on a layout where compaction moves another item, and two pointer moves land inside one `act()`, so the parent renders once for two moves and its layout is one step stale at release. The test expects release to apply the parent's layout, `RGL_W003` once, and one `onLayoutChange` carrying that final layout. A second case has the parent render every move: release commits the drag result with no `RGL_W003`. Both run in the React 18 and 19 cells.

### Comparisons

Two comparisons with different jobs:

```ts
// src/core/engine/compare.ts
type Match = "index" | "id"; // default "index"
/** Notification: geometry and flags. Ignores `data`. */
export function sameGeometry(a: Layout, b: Layout, match?: Match): boolean;
/** Sync gate: sameGeometry plus `data` by reference. */
export function sameInput(a: Layout, b: Layout, match?: Match): boolean;
```

`sameGeometry(a, b)` holds when:

- `a` and `b` have the same length and the same `i` at each index. With `match: "id"`, they hold the same set of ids instead, and each item is compared with the item of the same `i` through an id map;
- per item, the own keys other than `data` are the same set (`Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call` on both sides, so a key present as `undefined` differs from a missing key). `Object.hasOwn` is ES2022 and the repo compiles against the ES2020 `lib` (`tsconfig.json:4,39`);
- each value matches by SameValueZero (`x === y || (x !== x && y !== y)`);
- array-valued keys (`resizeHandles`, `constraints`) have the same length, and each element matches by SameValueZero.

SameValueZero, not `Object.is`, because that is what `fast-equals` does today. Measured in this revision with the installed `fast-equals` 4.0.3 (package.json:109): `deepEqual({x:0},{x:-0})` and `deepEqual({x:NaN},{x:NaN})` are both `true`, while `Object.is(0, -0)` is `false`.

`sameInput(a, b)` is `sameGeometry(a, b)` plus, per item, the same presence of `data` and `Object.is` on its value.

The flush comparison for `RGL_W003` (queue rule 5.3) passes `match: "id"`, so a consumer that keeps its layout in its own order does not warn for order alone. Every other use compares by index, as `deepEqual` does.

| Use                                          | Comparison            | Replaces                                                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------------------- | --------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Commit boundary against `lastNotifiedLayout` | `sameGeometry`        | `deepEqual` in the mount effect (`GridLayout.tsx:441-449`), the notify effect (`:491-514`) and the stop paths (`:625,799`) |
| Binding gate before `syncProps`              | `sameInput`           | the sync checks (`GridLayout.tsx:456,471`)                                                                                 |
| `RGL_W003` at the flush (queue rule 5.3)     | `sameGeometry`, by id | new                                                                                                                        |
| Responsive ingest gates, per breakpoint      | `sameInput`           | `ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:274`, `react/hooks/useResponsiveLayout.ts:273` (RGL-37)                                          |
| Responsive notification gate, per breakpoint | `sameGeometry`        | `react/hooks/useResponsiveLayout.ts:281` (RGL-37)                                                                          |

A prop change that only replaces an item's `data` fails `sameInput`, so the binding dispatches `syncProps` and the new reference reaches state, middleware and `renderItem`. It passes `sameGeometry`, so `onLayoutChange` does not fire. Mid-interaction, queue rule 3 patches the new `data` onto every engine layout at once, so `renderItem` sees it mid-drag; the layout itself is pending like any other and applies at release. Inline `data` objects therefore cannot cause a notify loop.

**The comparison keeps key-set semantics; BREAK 11 changes cloning.** `deepEqual` reports `{moved: false}` and `{}` as unequal because the key sets differ, and `sameGeometry` checks key sets the same way. It matches `deepEqual` on `0` versus `-0` and on `NaN`. Two differences remain by design: `data` is ignored, and constraint elements compare by reference. Factory constraints such as `aspectRatio()` already return a new object with new methods per call (`core/constraints.ts:234-242`), so `deepEqual` sees those as unequal too. A differential test runs `sameGeometry(a, b) === deepEqual(a, b)` over the same already-normalized layouts in the golden corpus and its JSON round trips, for every layout without `data`, with only the constraint-object exception. RX-17 has an explicit 3.0 golden diff: cloning preserves absent keys and drops `moved`, so it no longer creates a mount notification from normalization alone. The comparison change is RX-19 in §7. The differential includes the N-7 fixture from round 2: an item whose `constraints` is an inline literal `[{ name, constrainPosition: f }]` rebuilt each render around a module-level `f`. `deepEqual` calls two of those equal and `sameGeometry` does not, so an idle `syncProps` commits each time. The docs say to create constraint objects once, as for middleware.

RX-5 (double fire) is fixed in 2.4.0 already, in place (RGL-15). The engine keeps the fixed behavior.

### Store binding

```ts
// src/react/state/store.ts
export interface GridStore {
  getSnapshot(): GridEngineState;
  getServerSnapshot(): GridEngineState; // the initial state
  subscribe(listener: () => void): () => void;
  /** Reduces, notifies subscribers, emits, returns the events. */
  dispatch(action: GridAction): readonly GridEvent[];
}
export function createGridStore(
  initial: { state: GridEngineState; events: readonly GridEvent[] },
  emit: (events: readonly GridEvent[]) => void
): GridStore;

// React >= 18 native useSyncExternalStore; selector memoization is internal
export function useGridStore<T>(
  store: GridStore,
  selector: (s: GridEngineState) => T,
  isEqual?: (a: T, b: T) => boolean
): T;
```

- **Lifecycle.** The store lives in a ref, created once per mounted component on first render. It is never disposed or replaced. `subscribe` and `getSnapshot` are stable functions that read the store through the ref at call time. `useSyncExternalStore` owns the subscription: StrictMode's effect replay unsubscribes and resubscribes to the same store, with no render in between and nothing to recreate. On a real unmount the ref and the store are dropped together, and no callbacks fire. Timers and rAF loops belong to `GridItem` and binding effects, which clear them in their own cleanup.
- **Mount events.** `initial.events` holds the mount `layoutChange`, if any. The mount effect emits them once and sets a flag on the store. A replayed mount effect finds the flag set and emits nothing.
- **Callbacks.** `emit` reads the consumer's callbacks through a latest-value ref (`useLatest`), so inline callbacks work.
- **Selectors.** `GridLayout` selects the committed or working layout plus the placeholder, compared by reference. A same-cell move keeps both references, so it causes 0 `GridLayout` renders (T7).
- **Live pixels stay local.** The active item's pixel box is not in the store. It stays in the active `GridItem`, the mechanism #2304 added: a local revision counter re-renders only that item between snapped steps (`GridItem.tsx:251,553`).
- **Initial state** comes from `createInitialState`, synchronously, so the first render and `renderToString` show the synced, compacted layout.
- **Dispatch timing.** `syncProps` and `setConfig` dispatch from a layout effect, never during render. `emit` runs after the reducer returns, so a parent `setState` inside `onLayoutChange` never runs during render.
- **Test.** A StrictMode mount followed at once by a drag, with no parent rerender: each callback fires once, and `onLayoutChange` fires once at stop.
- **React floor.** 3.0 requires React `>= 18` and uses native `useSyncExternalStore`, with no shim package or tsup external entry. 2.4.x keeps `>= 16.8.0` and its existing hook state, with no store backport. The floor is package-wide, including `/legacy`.

### What the binding does

The binding is the only place that sees DOM events, elements and pixels.

1. **Pointer to grid.** On each pointer or scroll frame, it computes the grid-unit proposal from the client point (`getClientPoint`), the grid's client rect, its scroll offsets, the grab offset recorded at start and the transform scale. That is the input set `dropMath.ts` already uses for drops (`react/components/dropMath.ts:112-116`). Because the binding recomputes the proposal on every scrolled frame, edge scroll moves the item while the pointer holds still (RX-10d).
2. **Custom position strategies.** At drag start, when `positionStrategy.calcDragPosition` exists, the binding calls it with today's arguments: the client point and the pointer's offset inside the item. It converts the result as today: minus the parent rect divided by the scale, plus the parent scroll (`GridItem.tsx:402-418`). Each move adds the client-point delta since start, divided by the scale, to that start position. That is the sum RD's `deltaX`/`deltaY` give today, so mouse traces stay byte-identical. The existing call assertion stays (`test/spec/lifecycle-test.js:2465`), and a Playwright scenario drives the README's rotated strategy (`README.md:1028-1029`) and pins its per-frame boxes.
3. **Activation.** It runs the activation rule (§4, rank 8) before it dispatches `start`. Nothing reaches the reducer until the rule says "activate".
4. **Start.** It measures the container height once when `isBounded` and sends it on `start` (T9). If `dispatch` returns no `start` event (another interaction is active, the permission check failed, or middleware vetoed), the gesture goes to ignore-until-release.
5. **Ignore until release.** When the engine rejects a start or ends an interaction the gesture still holds (cancel, `itemRemoved`), the binding marks that item's RD or RR instance "ignore until release". Its handlers return early, and the item drops the `react-draggable-dragging` class and resets its local pixel box to the committed cell. The state clears on the first of: the adapter's next stop; `pointerup`, `pointercancel` or `blur` on `window`; or 30 s. The `window` listeners clear it on a deferred tick (`setTimeout(0)`) after the event, because pointer events dispatch before the compatibility `mouseup` that RD's capture-phase document listener turns into a stop (RD `DraggableCore.tsx:378-379`). A stop for which the binding holds no live id is dropped without a dispatch, so a release after a cancel never logs `RGL_W011`. This needs no upstream change, so it works on the RD 4.5.0 floor.
6. **Callbacks.** It maps each `interaction` event onto today's positional callbacks. It joins in the DOM values the reducer never sees: argument 5 is the last native event the binding saw for that gesture (a `KeyboardEvent` for a nudge, which fits `event: Event` at `core/types.ts:232`), argument 6 is the item node (`GridLayout.tsx:617` passes `data.node` today), and argument 7 is the `InteractionDetail`. For `kind: "drop"`, no engine event maps to a drag callback. Hover uses `dropConfig.onDragOver` or `onTouchDragOver`, commit maps to `onDrop`, and cancel fires neither `onDrop` nor a drag callback. Today's simulated drag callbacks (`GridItem.tsx:799-833`) survive only through the private legacy adapter (BREAK 2).
7. **Errors.** It routes `error` events to `onError` when passed, and to `console.error` once per source and grid otherwise, in production too (§4, rank 4; §15).
8. On release, `GridLayout` dispatches `stop{interactionId}` without `to`. The reducer re-runs the pipeline on `lastProposal`, the last accepted proposal, so a release after a rejected move commits the last accepted step instead of re-running the rejected proposal at `phase: "stop"`, where a middleware `false` would cancel the whole drag.
9. A dedicated layout effect with empty deps dispatches `cancel("pointercancel")` when it re-runs and finds `active` set. It re-runs only when a hidden subtree is shown again (Suspense, `<Activity mode="hidden">`) and on a StrictMode replay, where `active` is `null`. The config and props effect never dispatches this cancel, because it re-runs on every controlled layout update. The only cancel it dispatches is the `interactionKey` one below, and only when the key changed.

### The responsive cancel channel

The store is internal to `GridLayout`, so `ResponsiveGridLayout` cannot dispatch into it. `GridLayout` takes one new optional prop for this, `interactionKey?: string | number`. In the layout effect that dispatches config and props, the binding compares `interactionKey` first. When it changed while an interaction is active, the binding dispatches `cancel{reason: "breakpointChange"}`, and only then `setConfig` and `syncProps`. So the drag never continues under the new column count.

`ResponsiveGridLayout` passes its current breakpoint as the key. The prop exists for breakpoint changes, which is why its cancel reason is `"breakpointChange"`, and its docs say so. A consumer who composes `useResponsiveLayout` with `GridLayout` passes `interactionKey={breakpoint}` and gets the same behavior. Without the key, a breakpoint change mid-drag queues like any other prop change.

Order, from the code: `ResponsiveGridLayout` fires `onBreakpointChange`, `onLayoutChange` and `onWidthChange` in a passive effect that also sets its state (`ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:329,399,416`). `GridLayout` sees the new key on the next render. So the cancelled `onDragStop` comes after those three callbacks. RGL-24 pins this order with a test. Its argument 1 is therefore the old breakpoint's layout, and the docs say that argument 1 of a cancelled stop is not a layout to persist (RGL-41).

### `useGridLayout` 3.0 contract

The hook uses the same store as the component (RGL-25). Its current observer-like action names, duplicate collision option and React element in drop state are public (`react/hooks/useGridLayout.ts:49-113`). BREAK 8 replaces them with this contract. All actions use grid units; pixels and React elements belong to the caller's binding.

| 2.x member                                                   | 3.0 member                                           | Engine path                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `layout`, `setLayout` (`:71-73`)                             | `layout`, `setLayout`                                | Select working or committed layout; validate then dispatch `setLayout`                                                    |
| `dragState`, `resizeState`, `dropState` (`:75-79`)           | `active`                                             | Readonly selected interaction: kind, id, start item/layout, placeholder and working layout; no DOM node or pixel position |
| `onDragStart` (`:81`)                                        | `startDrag(itemId, at?)`                             | `start{kind:"drag"}`, then an optional initial proposal; returns interaction id or `null`                                 |
| `onDrag` (`:83`)                                             | `moveDrag(interactionId, to)`                        | `propose`                                                                                                                 |
| `onDragStop` (`:85`)                                         | `stopDrag(interactionId, to?)`                       | `stop`, one final pipeline run                                                                                            |
| `onResizeStart` (`:87`)                                      | `startResize(itemId, handle)`                        | `start{kind:"resize"}`; returns interaction id or `null`                                                                  |
| `onResize` (`:89-95`)                                        | `moveResize(interactionId, to)`                      | `propose`, including optional n/w origin                                                                                  |
| `onResizeStop` (`:97`)                                       | `stopResize(interactionId, to?)`                     | `stop`; absent origin keeps the last accepted origin                                                                      |
| `onDropDragOver` (`:99-102`)                                 | `startDrop(item, at)`, `moveDrop(interactionId, to)` | Explicit drop start followed by `propose`; start returns id or `null`                                                     |
| `onDropDragLeave` (`:104`)                                   | `cancel(interactionId, reason)`                      | `cancel`, including `dropLeft`                                                                                            |
| `onDrop` (`:106`)                                            | `stopDrop(interactionId, dropId)`                    | `stop` with final item id                                                                                                 |
| `preventCollision` option (`:62`)                            | `compactor.preventCollision`                         | One source of collision policy                                                                                            |
| `containerHeight`, `isInteracting`, `compactor` (`:108-112`) | unchanged                                            | Derived from selected layout, `active` and config                                                                         |

Callers pass the id returned by start, so stale and foreign actions hit the engine's identity rule. Notification is commit-only (RX-9). The migration guide shows the semantic rewrite; the codemod flags these calls for review rather than guessing ids or pixel ownership.

`+` is new, `~` is changed, `-` is deleted. The release is in brackets. Unmarked files stay as they are.
