### The boundary to react-draggable and react-resizable

Today `GridItem` talks to both libraries directly (`GridItem.tsx:898-932`) and works around them in four places:

- It prefixes `cancel` with `.react-resizable-handle` so handles do not start a drag (`GridItem.tsx:925`).
- It casts native events to `MouseEvent`. That hides the touch bug RX-7 (`GridItem.tsx:405-411,440-444`).
- It fakes `DraggableData` to simulate a drag for external drops (`GridItem.tsx:783-837`).
- It reads `nativeEvent ?? e` to work around the event type (`GridItem.tsx:717`, #2264).

  3.0.0 replaces these with **internal translation functions**, not interfaces. There is one implementation of each, so an interface would be a pass-through. Interfaces come with the pointer-events experiment (Later), once a second implementation exists.

```ts
// src/react/gesture/pointer.ts
export function getClientPoint(
  e: MouseEvent | TouchEvent,
  touchId?: number
): { clientX: number; clientY: number } | null;

// src/react/gesture/fromDraggable.ts
export function dragProposal(
  point: { clientX: number; clientY: number },
  frame: GridFrame // rect, scroll, grab offset, scale, PositionParams
): DragProposal;

// src/react/gesture/fromResizable.ts
export function resizeProposal(
  handle: ResizeHandleAxis,
  point: { clientX: number; clientY: number },
  frame: GridFrame
): ResizeProposal;
```

Rules at the boundary:

1. **RGL computes sizes from client coordinates.** It does not use react-resizable's `size`. Today the two models overlap: RR keeps `lastSize`/`slack`/`lastHandleRect` and corrects w/n handles through `getBoundingClientRect` on the handle (`Resizable.tsx:23-32,103-118`), while RGL recomputes left and top itself through `resizeItemInDirection` (rgl-react §6.2). RR stays for handle rendering and gesture capture.
2. The selector that keeps resize handles from starting a drag stays a local constant beside its one use. It is not exported.
3. **External drop and keyboard dispatch engine actions directly.** Nothing fakes `DraggableData`.
4. **Contract tests** (RGL-05) pin what the translation functions read from RD and RR, as structural subsets, so additive upstream fields do not break the floor-versus-latest cells (§8).

---

## 4. Extension Points

Every extension point below ships in **3.0.0** or later. 2.4.0 has none.

### Ranking

Ranked by what each point unblocks, then by the demand found in the pluggability survey. "Demand" lists only issue and PR numbers that appear in the audits. Demand marked `[title]` was seen in search results only, not read.

| Rank | Extension point                                           | Layer                         | Ships in                    | Demand                                                                  |
| ---- | --------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1    | Engine (prerequisite, internal)                           | core                          | 3.0.0                       | #345, #977, #459 blocker (pluggability §3.0)                            |
| 2    | `Compactor.resolveDrag`                                   | core                          | 3.0.0                       | #2213, #1964, #2023, #1226, #1139, #346                                 |
| 3    | `compact(layout, cols, ctx)` context arg                  | core                          | 3.0.0                       | #2213, #346 ("hooking into the compactor")                              |
| 4    | Interaction middleware (`onProposal`, `onResolved`)       | core, surfaced by React       | 3.0.0                       | #345, #977, #1979, #1966                                                |
| 5    | `LayoutItem.data` passthrough                             | core                          | 3.0.0                       | #929, #458 [title]                                                      |
| 6    | `renderPlaceholder`                                       | React                         | 3.0.0                       | #1426, #449                                                             |
| 7    | `renderItem`                                              | React                         | 3.0.0                       | #1241, #299, #506, #346                                                 |
| 8    | Activation rules (distance, or delay + tolerance)         | React binding, pure core math | 3.0.0                       | PR #1607, RD #350                                                       |
| 9    | Keyboard: `nudge` action + `keyboard` prop                | core + React                  | 3.0.0                       | #1907, #936, #1795, #346 (tambaqui)                                     |
| 10   | `CollisionStrategy`                                       | core                          | Later (3.x+)                | #2213, #1964 (same threads as rank 2; no demand of its own)             |
| 11   | RTL direction mapping                                     | core                          | Later (3.x+)                | #1794, #2002, PR #875, RR #95                                           |
| 12   | Transition hooks                                          | React                         | recipe in 3.0.0, prop later | #571, #286, #103, #180                                                  |
| 13   | Multi-grid transfer                                       | core + React provider         | Later (3.x+)                | #11, #459, #792, #334, PR #2260; group drag #322, #1119, PR #2267       |
| 14   | Injectable store; public drag, resize and drop interfaces | React                         | Later (3.x+)                | **No demand found** (pluggability §2). RFC 0001 OQ2 (`rfcs/0001:1598`). |

Points 10-14 depend on the engine. No 3.0.0 point depends on them.

Three items from revision 1 are no longer extension points. The edge-scroll fixes (RX-10a-d) ship with today's constants and no config surface. The drop paths are extracted without a source registry. `itemComponent` is deleted, because renderItem covers its demand without retaining the existing public GridItemProps export in 3.0 (BREAK 3).

### How the points compose

```
 propose (grid units, from the binding)
   |
   +-> constraints[] then item.constraints[]   (RFC 0002, unchanged)
   +-> middleware.onProposal   rank 4  (veto, or edit; an edit
   |                                    re-runs constraints)
   +-> drag / drop / keyboard move:
   |     compactor.resolveDrag              rank 2
   |   resize:
   |     resizeElement (n/w anchoring, collisions)
   +-> compactor.compact(l, cols, ctx)       rank 3
   +-> middleware.onResolved   rank 4  (veto, or replace the layout)
   +-> move: workingLayout + placeholder (6), items (7)
       stop: the same stages once more on the last proposal,
             then commit -> layoutChange (at most once)
```

Order is fixed and documented. Each stage takes the previous stage's output and nothing else, which makes each stage testable alone. `stop` runs the full pipeline once more on the last proposal (constraints, `onProposal`, `resolveDrag` or `resizeElement`, `compact`, `onResolved`) and commits that result (§3). So `phase: "stop"` reaches every stage, and a compactor that skips work during `move` still compacts at stop. Activation (rank 8) runs in the binding before `start` is dispatched, so it is not a pipeline stage.

### 1. Engine (prerequisite)

Specified in §3. Listed here because every point below plugs into it. Its own test is the reducer table (§11): an action sequence in, layout and events out, no React.

### 2. `Compactor.resolveDrag`

**Gap.** `moveElement` runs before the compactor and switches on `compactType` (`core/layout.ts:296-301,383-384,436`). A compactor that wants swap or insert-reorder semantics during a drag cannot get them (rgl-core X1). In #2213 a user reports that `onMove` on a custom compactor is never called (pluggability §2).

```ts
export interface DragContext {
  readonly layout: Layout; // working layout before this step
  readonly item: LayoutItem; // the moving item, as in ctx.layout
  readonly x: number; // target, after constraints and onProposal
  readonly y: number;
  readonly phase: InteractionPhase;
  readonly kind: "drag" | "drop"; // resize never comes here
  readonly source: InteractionSource; // "keyboard" for nudges
  readonly cols: number;
  readonly maxRows: number;
  readonly preventCollision: boolean;
  readonly allowOverlap: boolean;
  readonly startItem: LayoutItem;
  readonly startLayout: Layout;
}

export interface Compactor {
  readonly name: string; // open identity for third-party compactors
  readonly allowOverlap: boolean;
  readonly preventCollision?: boolean;
  compact(layout: Layout, cols: number, ctx?: CompactContext): Layout;
  /**
   * Resolve collisions when ctx.item moves to (x, y). Must not mutate.
   * Return null to reject the step.
   */
  resolveDrag(ctx: DragContext): Layout | null;
}

export type DragAxis = "x" | "y" | null; // null: free-form swap
export function classicResolveDrag(
  ctx: DragContext,
  axis: DragAxis,
  opts?: { readonly order?: "rowCol" | "colRow" } // default from axis
): Layout | null;
```

- **Default.** Each built-in compactor sets `resolveDrag` explicitly, through the same helper a third party would call. `verticalCompactor` and `fastVerticalCompactor` use `ctx => classicResolveDrag(ctx, "y")`. `horizontalCompactor` and `fastHorizontalCompactor` use `"x"`. `noCompactor` uses `ctx => classicResolveDrag(ctx, null)`, the free-form swap; it does not return `null`. The single `noOverlapCompactor` skips collision resolution and compaction, preserving positions. A custom `{...c, allowOverlap:true}` must honor that same meaning. This is the 3.0 contract; it changes both fast overlap variants, which require manual migration (§12), while the core and wrap overlap variants already skip compaction. `wrapCompactor` uses `classicResolveDrag(ctx, "x", { order: "rowCol" })`, because wrap sorts row-col (`core/sort.ts:32-34`) but pushes horizontally (`core/interaction/resolveDrag.ts:95-100` at `2182f06a`). These reproduce today's `moveElement` cascade on a working copy the helper owns. `moveElement` and `moveElementAwayFromCollision` are removed from public barrels in 3.0 (§12).
- **Required resolver.** 3.0 has no `type` fallback or 2.3-style custom-compactor fixture. Custom authors rename `type` to `name` and add `resolveDrag`, for example `ctx => classicResolveDrag(ctx, "y")`. A missing resolver is a compile error. The migration guide covers JavaScript consumers too.

- **Reject is explicit.** `null` means "reject this step". It is not inferred from an unchanged layout, because a normal stop in the same cell also returns an unchanged layout. On a move, `null` drops the step. On a stop, the last accepted working layout commits, as `preventCollision` behaves today (`core/layout.ts:317-322`).
- **Resize never calls `resolveDrag`.** Resize runs `resizeElement` instead (§3 pipeline). A swap compactor therefore cannot turn a resize into a swap.
- **Composition.** The output goes to `compact`.
- **Test in isolation.** Pure Jest: `resolveDrag(ctx)` against golden fixtures. `extras/swapCompactor.ts` ships with its own spec, as executable documentation, the way `wrapCompactor` does today.
- **Name.** No `onMove` field exists in `src/`, the README or the examples today (grep). There is no existing name to alias.
- **Open identities.** `Compactor.name` and `PositionStrategy.name` are strings. `PositionStrategy.itemClassName?: string` supplies the class; built-in transforms set `"cssTransforms"`. Today the closed strategy type (`core/types.ts:318`) feeds the class through `GridLayout.tsx:387` and `GridItem.tsx:882`, whose CSS selects transform transitions (`css/styles.css:13-15`). 3.0 removes the mount-time class flip (RX-14) without renaming shipped classes.

- RGL-35 exports `classicResolveDrag` and the `DragContext` and `CompactContext` types from `src/core/index.ts`, next to the existing helpers for custom compactors (`core/index.ts:130-133`), and `swapCompactor` from `src/extras/index.ts`. A consumer fixture compiled against the built package imports each of them.
- **Change detection.** 2.4 compares `type`, `allowOverlap`, `preventCollision` and `compact` by value/reference (RGL-17). 3.0 compares `name` instead of `type` and adds the required `resolveDrag` reference (§3). An inline spread with the same fields is stable. The public `getCompactor` is removed; legacy retains a private mapping. Today its `preventCollision` branch creates a new object (`core/compactors.ts:221-223`).

### 3. Compact context argument

**Gap.** `compact(layout, cols)` (`core/types.ts:295`) cannot see which item is active, the phase, or the start layout. So a compactor cannot freeze the dragged item or compact only on stop (rgl-core X2).

```ts
export interface CompactContext {
  readonly activeId?: string;
  readonly phase?: InteractionPhase | "sync";
  readonly kind?: InteractionKind;
  readonly source?: InteractionSource;
  readonly maxRows: number;
  readonly startLayout?: Layout;
}
```

- **Default.** Built-in compactors ignore it, so output is byte-identical.
- **Composition.** The optional third argument is safe: existing two-argument compactors ignore it.
- **Test.** Pure Jest: a compactor that skips `activeId` while `phase === "move"`, asserted against fixtures.

### 4. Interaction middleware

**Gap.** Callbacks only observe. There is no veto and no transform, and the pre-compaction layout is never exposed (pluggability §1; #345). The fix also covers cancel or revert on stop (#1966) and mutation in stop (#1979).

```ts
export type DragEdit = { readonly x?: number; readonly y?: number };
export type ResizeEdit = { readonly w?: number; readonly h?: number };
export type ProposalResult = void | false | DragEdit | ResizeEdit;
export type ResolvedResult = void | false | Layout;

export interface InteractionMiddleware {
  readonly name: string; // used in errors, warnings and rejectedBy
  /** After constraints. start, move and stop. */
  onProposal?(d: InteractionDetail): ProposalResult;
  /** After compaction. move and stop. */
  onResolved?(d: InteractionDetail): ResolvedResult;
}

// GridLayout and useGridLayout props:
//   middleware?: readonly InteractionMiddleware[]
//   onError?: (error: unknown, source: string, detail?: InteractionDetail)
//     => void
```

Both results are bare values: an edit object, or a `Layout` array. `false` vetoes, `undefined` passes.

- **`onProposal`** runs after constraints, at `start`, `move` and `stop`. At `start`, `d.proposed` is the start item. At a drag or resize `start` only a `false` veto counts: no step runs there, so a returned edit is ignored with the dev warning `RGL_W012`. A drop start runs a step, so its edit applies. A drag or drop edit may set `x` and `y`; a resize edit may set `w` and `h`. Any other field is ignored, with warning `RGL_W005`. An edit is validated first (finite, rounded to integers, inside the §14 bounds), then the constraints run again, then validation runs once more. So middleware cannot hand a fractional or non-finite value to a compactor, the input that makes `fastHorizontalCompactor` throw (C-B4). For `n` and `w` handles, `x` and `y` are re-anchored after every size change, so the opposite edge stays fixed.
- **`onResolved`** runs after compaction, at `move` and `stop`. A returned layout is validated (same id set, finite integers, inside bounds) and used without a second compaction. An invalid result emits warning `RGL_W005`, in production too, and counts as a veto for that step.
- **Composition.** Array order. The first `false` stops the chain and vetoes the step. A returned edit or layout feeds the next middleware. Middleware runs after constraints, so constraints stay the place for geometric clamping (RFC 0002).
- **Veto semantics per phase.**
  - `start`: no interaction begins. `active` stays `null`. No `onDragStart` fires. The binding ignores the gesture until release.
  - `move`: the step is dropped. State stays at the last accepted step.
  - `stop`: cancel semantics (below), with `cancelReason: "vetoed"`. That reverts the whole drag, unlike a `null` from `resolveDrag`. The docs (RGL-41) carry a recipe for a forbidden-zone middleware that keeps the item at its last valid position on release: at `stop`, return an edit back to the item's cell in `d.layoutBefore` instead of `false`.
- **Errors.** A throw from middleware, `resolveDrag` or `compact` is caught by the reducer and returned as an `error` event. The binding calls `onError` if passed. Without `onError`, it calls `console.error` once per source and grid, in production too, with the strategy name and phase; a throwing strategy is a third-party bug, and swallowing it silently leaves an item that will not move. Inside an interaction the step counts as a veto, and at `stop` that means cancel semantics with `cancelReason: "error"`. Outside an interaction (phase `"sync"`: initial state, `syncProps`, `setConfig`, `setLayout`) the previous state is kept (§3). Nothing is rethrown.
- **Start/stop pairing.** Every `onDragStart` and `onResizeStart` gets exactly one matching stop callback. A cancel or a stop veto fires `onDragStop`/`onResizeStop` with `(startLayout, startItem, startItem, null, e, el, detail)`, where `detail.cancelled` is `true` and `detail.cancelReason` says why. There is no separate cancel callback: argument 7 carries the whole story. Consumers that ignore it see a no-op drag that ends where it started. `startLayout` never contains an item removed mid-interaction (§3 queue rules 1 and 2). Only unmounting the grid skips callbacks. The docs say that argument 1 of a cancelled stop is not a layout to persist; after a `breakpointChange` cancel it is the old breakpoint's layout (§3). External drops fire no drag callbacks in 3.0, on entry, move, cancel or commit, so the pairing invariant is total for public drag/resize callbacks (RX-11a, resolved OQ6). Legacy retains its v1 drop callback sequence through a private mapping.
- **Callback detail.** Every drag and resize callback gets an optional seventh argument, the `InteractionDetail` for that step. Positional arguments 1-6 keep today's values. This answers #345 without changing a signature.
- **Test.** Pure engine tests: a vetoing middleware leaves `state.layout` unchanged; a replacing middleware's layout is committed without a second compaction; an edit with `x: 1.5` is rounded and clamped; a resize edit's `x` is ignored with `RGL_W005`; an edit returned at a drag `start` is ignored with `RGL_W012`; a throw becomes an `error` event, at `move`, at `stop` and at `"sync"`. RTL tests confirm the seventh argument reaches `onDragStop`, and that a throw without `onError` logs once under `NODE_ENV=production`.

### 5. `LayoutItem.data` passthrough

**Gap.** `cloneLayoutItem` copies a fixed list of 16 keys, so custom fields vanish on every compaction (`core/layout.ts:81-99`, verified with `data`; rgl-core §1.2). In #929 the maintainer's answer was "store a map elsewhere" (pluggability §2).

```ts
export interface LayoutItem<TData = unknown> {
  // ...existing fields
  readonly data?: TData;
}
export type Layout<TData = unknown> = readonly LayoutItem<TData>[];
// The parameter is threaded through every type that carries a layout:
// GridLayoutProps<TData>, GridLayout<TData>, EventCallback<TData>,
// ResponsiveGridLayoutProps<B, TData>, ResponsiveLayouts<B, TData>,
// useGridLayout<TData>, useResponsiveLayout<B, TData>.
```

- **Default.** `cloneLayoutItem` copies `data` **by reference** and adds no other keys. The whitelist stays, but only present keys are copied and `moved` is removed (BREAK 11). That matters because `fast-equals.deepEqual({minW: undefined}, {})` is `false` (rgl-core §1.0). `Layout` is already a readonly array today (`core/types.ts:113`), and 3.0 separately makes its item fields readonly (BREAK 5).
- **Sync and notification.** `sameInput` compares `data` by reference, so a data-only prop change reaches state. `sameGeometry` ignores it, so that change fires no `onLayoutChange` (§3). The responsive `deepEqual` paths move in the same PR, compared per breakpoint: the ingest gates (`ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:274`, `useResponsiveLayout.ts:273`) to `sameInput`, and the notification gate (`useResponsiveLayout.ts:281`) to `sameGeometry`. None of them walks `data`.
- **Generic callbacks.** `onLayoutChange?: (layout: Layout) => void` (`GridLayout.tsx:158`) is a function-typed property, so a `(l: Layout<MyData>) => void` handler fails under `strictFunctionTypes` unless the props are generic. The same holds for every `EventCallback` and the responsive and hook types, which is why the parameter is threaded through all of them.
- **Composition.** Opaque to every strategy. Middleware and `renderItem` can read it.
- **Test.** Pure: `cloneLayoutItem`, `compact`, `resolveDrag` and `sync` keep `data` by reference. Consumer compile fixtures pass a `Layout<MyData>` handler to `onLayoutChange`, `onDragStop` and the responsive `onLayoutChange` (§11). RTL: a data-only prop change reaches `renderItem` on `GridLayout`, `ResponsiveGridLayout` and `useResponsiveLayout`, idle and mid-drag, with no `onLayoutChange`. The hook cases run through its props and through both setters, with a cyclic `data` object.

### 6. `renderPlaceholder`

**Gap.** `renderPlaceholder()` always renders a `GridItem` with class `react-grid-placeholder` and an empty `<div />` (`GridLayout.tsx:1271-1299`). The workaround in #1426 is `ReactDOM.render` into the placeholder node at drag start.

```ts
export interface PlaceholderRenderProps {
  readonly item: LayoutItem; // grid units
  readonly position: Position; // px box
  readonly kind: InteractionKind;
}
// GridLayout props:
//   renderPlaceholder?: (p: PlaceholderRenderProps) => React.ReactNode;
//   placeholderClassName?: string;
```

- **Default.** `GridPlaceholder`, which renders today's markup.
- **Composition.** RGL keeps owning the positioned wrapper. The function renders inside it. Returning `null` hides the placeholder but keeps the wrapper.
- **Test.** The logic is pure (wrapper geometry comes from `calcGridItemPosition`). One RTL test: drive a drag with the shared driver and assert the slot got `{kind: "drag"}` and its output sits inside `.react-grid-placeholder`.

### 7. `renderItem`

**Gap.** The child contract (forward the ref, `style`, `className`, mouse and touch handlers, render `{children}`) is the largest support load (#1241, #299). #506 asks for the computed size in children.

```ts
export interface ItemRenderState {
  readonly item: LayoutItem;
  readonly position: Position;
  readonly state: "idle" | "dragging" | "resizing" | "dropping";
}
// GridLayout prop:
//   renderItem?: (child: React.ReactElement, s: ItemRenderState)
//     => React.ReactNode;
```

- **Default.** RGL renders its own wrapper `<div>` even with `renderItem` unset, and puts the child inside unchanged, including its ref. This is BREAK 12, adopted with the lowest confidence and a panel question (§13). Legacy keeps the clone contract.
- **Where it runs.** `GridLayout` calls `renderItem(child, state)` during its own render, for every item, and passes the result as `GridItem`'s children. RGL renders its own wrapper `<div>` with the position style, classes, handlers and resize handles. The user's content needs no ref forwarding. This applies to every 3.0 item, with `renderItem` customizing only the wrapper's content.
- **`className` and `style`.** RGL owns wrapper classes, geometry, handlers and handles. The child keeps its own `className`, `style` and ref. `renderItem` receives that unchanged child. Existing child classes, including styled-components classes, stay on the inner element. Content may need `height: 100%`; selectors that assumed the child was the positioned item need migration. RGL-38 tests refs and nested interactive content.
- **No cache.** There is no per-item element cache. A cache keyed on anything less than every input `renderItem` reads would show stale content, and `position` alone changes with every width change. A new element per render means `GridItem` re-renders whenever `GridLayout` renders with `renderItem` set. `GridLayout` itself renders only on a layout or placeholder change (T7) or a parent render. Consumers who need fewer renders memoize the content component (`React.memo`) or the function. T8 is measured on grids without `renderItem`.
- **Position during a gesture.** `position` is the cell-snapped box. The live sub-cell box of the active item stays local to `GridItem` (§3).
- **Composition.** A transition library can wrap items through `renderItem` (rank 12).
- **Test.** RTL: a spy `renderItem` receives `state: "dragging"` mid-drag; a plain function component (no forwardRef) works as content; a width change passes the new `position`; a closed-over parent value that changes shows up in idle items on the next render. Pure: the `ItemRenderState` derivation is a selector over engine state.

### 8. Activation rules

**Gap.** `dragConfig.threshold` covers distance only (`core/types.ts:498`). RGL reimplements the threshold FSM inside `GridItem` (`GridItem.tsx:437-448,492-527`), and on touch the FSM is skipped entirely because `clientX` is undefined (RX-7). PR #1607 (`delayedDragOnTouch`, 26 comments) and RD #350 (25 comments) ask for delay.

```ts
export type ActivationRule =
  | { readonly distance: number }
  | { readonly delay: number; readonly tolerance: number };

export interface DragConfig {
  // ...existing fields; `threshold` stays and maps to { distance }
  readonly activation?:
    | ActivationRule
    | { readonly mouse?: ActivationRule; readonly touch?: ActivationRule };
}

// core/interaction/activation.ts: numbers in, no DOM types
export function decideActivation(
  rule: ActivationRule,
  dx: number,
  dy: number,
  elapsedMs: number
): "activate" | "wait" | "cancel";
```

- **Data only.** There is no predicate function in 3.0.0. A rule is `{distance}` or `{delay, tolerance}`.
- **Where it runs.** In the binding, before `start` is dispatched. Nothing reaches the reducer until the rule says `activate`. A pure click therefore never adds the `react-draggable-dragging` class (`GridItem.tsx:447`).
- **Default.** `{ distance: threshold }` (3 in v2, 0 in legacy). The default path is unchanged: a distance of 0 activates on the start sample itself, as `if (dragThreshold > 0)` does today (`GridItem.tsx:439`), and the comparison keeps today's `distance < dragThreshold` wait (`GridItem.tsx:498`).
- **Delay.** A timer starts at pointer-down and calls `decideActivation` with the current offset when it fires, so a stationary long-press activates without a move event. Moving past `tolerance` before the timer cancels the rule.
- **Touch delay and native scroll.** `preventDefault` only works when called synchronously while the browser dispatches a cancelable event. A timer callback cannot call it. With a delay rule on touch, the binding passes `allowMobileScroll` to RD, so RD does not call `preventDefault` on `touchstart` (installed RD 4.5.0, `node_modules/react-draggable/build/cjs/DraggableCore.js:105`). The binding adds its own non-passive `touchmove` listener. It calls `preventDefault` there while the delay is pending and the finger is within `tolerance`, and on every `touchmove` after activation. Once the finger passes `tolerance`, the listener stops calling it and the rule cancels. A browser may decide scroll ownership on the first `touchmove`, so a cancelled delay can still lose native scroll for that one touch; the verification below records which.
- **Verification.** Automation cannot cover this check. Script-dispatched touch events are untrusted and never scroll, and CDP `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` does not fire DOM touch events in headless Chromium, as the repo already records (`test/e2e/touch-drop.spec.ts:30-31`). So the native-scroll behavior is checked by hand on iOS Safari and Android Chrome in the 3.0.0 rc window, as a line in the rc checklist (§15). Jest with fake timers covers the listener logic.
- **Veto after activation.** If middleware vetoes the `start` that activation dispatched, RD's start callback has already returned (`DraggableCore.js:128` checks `false` only during that call). The binding ends the gesture through the ignore-until-release state. No consumer stop fires, because no start fired.
- **Sub-threshold deltas.** On activation, the binding replays the full delta from the start sample. Today those deltas are discarded, so the item lags the pointer by up to `threshold` px (rgl-react §4.12). Listed as RX-12.
- **Test.** Pure `decideActivation` tests with fake times. jsdom tests with fake timers for a touch long-press and for `preventDefault` calls on `touchmove` within and past `tolerance`. The manual device check above.

### 9. Keyboard

**Gap.** Nothing exists (pluggability §1). Asked for in #1907, #936, #1795 and in #346 by tambaqui.

```ts
// The GridAction member (§3):
//   { type: "nudge"; itemId: string; delta: NudgeDelta }
// The reducer's nudge step lives in core/engine/reduce.ts (internal).

// GridLayout prop:
//   keyboard?: boolean | {
//     moveKeys?: Partial<Record<string, readonly [number, number]>>;
//     resizeModifier?: "shift" | "alt";
//     cancelOnEscape?: boolean; // default true when keyboard is on
//     announce?: (message: string) => void;
//   }
```

- **Default.** Off. With `keyboard: true`, arrow keys move the focused item and shift plus arrows resize it.
- **One mechanism.** A key press dispatches one `nudge` action. The reducer checks movability, then runs start, one step and stop through the same pipeline as a pointer: a move nudge goes through `resolveDrag`, a resize nudge through `resizeElement`, both with constraints and middleware and `source: "keyboard"`. Keyboard and pointer moves cannot diverge. There is no batch API.
- **During a pointer interaction.** A `nudge` is a no-op while `active` is set (§3), so a key press cannot move or stop someone else's drag.
- **Commit.** Each key press commits at once. A rejected press (the permission check, a veto, a `null` from `resolveDrag`, a throw) commits nothing and fires no callbacks (§3).
- **Callbacks.** A nudge fires the drag trio (`onDragStart`, `onDrag`, `onDragStop`) or the resize trio, with `detail.source: "keyboard"` and the `KeyboardEvent` as argument 5.
- **Where the handler lives.** With a roving `tabIndex`, focus sits on items, so the key handler is on each item wrapper. It ignores events whose `target !== currentTarget` or that are `defaultPrevented`, so arrow keys in an `<input>` inside an item never move it.
- **Escape.** Escape cancels an active pointer interaction only when `keyboard` is on (`cancelOnEscape`). It is never on by default. Focus can be on `body` during a mouse drag, so the binding listens for Escape on the document while an interaction is active.
- **Accessibility.** One tab stop for the grid, with a roving `tabIndex` across items. Each item gets `aria-describedby` pointing at instructions, with SSR-stable ids from React 18's `useId`. Moves are announced through a live region, written with `textContent`. There is no `aria-grabbed`, which WAI-ARIA 1.1 deprecated.
- **Not exported in 3.0.0.** A `useGridKeyboard` hook would need the store, which stays internal. It waits for the public store (rank 14).
- **Test.** Pure reducer matrix for `nudge`: bounds, statics, both permission override directions, collisions, `preventCollision`, a rejected nudge that emits no interaction events, and a nudge while a drag is active. RTL with `@testing-library/user-event` (a new dev dependency): assert `onLayoutChange`, the announce text, and that an arrow key inside an `<input>` does nothing.

### 10. `CollisionStrategy` (Later)

Deferred: it has no demand of its own (the demand column repeats rank 2), and the fast compactors would ignore it. RGL-09 (#2322) left the three `collides` copies where they are, because their NaN and same-`i` semantics differ (§3). Unifying them is a separate tagged golden diff (L6). A pluggable strategy can follow once a `resolveDrag` author asks for one; `collides` is already exported for them.

### 11. RTL direction mapping (Later)

**Gap.** v2's positioning, drag deltas and compaction are all LTR-anchored (#1794 triage, pluggability §2). `src/` has no `dir`, `rtl` or `direction` handling (rgl-tests §3.2).

```ts
export interface GridDirection {
  readonly dir: "ltr" | "rtl";
  toLogicalX(screenLeft: number, width: number, containerWidth: number): number;
  toScreenX(logicalLeft: number, width: number, containerWidth: number): number;
  mapHandle(h: ResizeHandleAxis): ResizeHandleAxis; // e <-> w in rtl
}
// gridConfig.direction?: "ltr" | "rtl" | GridDirection
```

- **Composition.** Layouts stay logical (x = 0 is inline-start). Only the binding's pixel-to-grid step flips, so compactors, constraints and middleware need no change. The direction also maps resize handles before `resizeElement` sees them.
- **Why later.** It needs the 3.0.0 binding first, and an RTL golden suite does not exist yet.
- **Test.** Pure round trip over integer px: `toLogicalX(toScreenX(x)) === x` over the calc fixtures. Handle mapping table. One RTL render test asserting the transform output.

### 12. Transition hooks

**Gap.** Enter and exit animation is impossible today (#571, #286). Initial-render animation (#103) has only a CSS-class workaround.

- **3.0.0: recipe, no new API.** `renderItem` (rank 7) receives `state`, so a consumer can wrap items with a motion library. The item wrapper gets `data-rgl-state="idle|dragging|resizing|dropping"`. Ship an example.
- **Later: `transitions` prop.**

```ts
// GridLayout prop (later):
//   transitions?: { enterMs?: number; exitMs?: number; skipInitial?: boolean }
// Item wrapper gets data-rgl-state="entering|idle|exiting".
```

- **Composition.** An exiting item stays mounted for `exitMs` with `static: true` semantics in the engine, so nothing collides with a ghost. It is removed from the committed layout at once.
- **Test.** RTL with fake timers: a removed child stays in the DOM with `data-rgl-state="exiting"` for `exitMs`, then unmounts.

### 13. Multi-grid transfer (Later)

**Gap.** This is the oldest open demand, from 2015 (#11). In #459 the blocker was "starting/stopping dragging without sending fake mouseup/down" (pluggability §2). The engine removes that blocker: a target grid accepts a foreign item through `start{kind:"drop"}`, `propose` and `stop`, with no faked events.

```ts
export interface GridDropTarget {
  readonly id: string;
  getRect(): DOMRect;
  toGrid(clientX: number, clientY: number): { x: number; y: number };
  canAccept?(item: LayoutItem, fromGridId: string): boolean;
}
export interface GridTransferSession {
  readonly item: LayoutItem;
  readonly group?: readonly string[]; // group drag: same delta for each
  readonly sourceGridId: string;
  readonly overGridId: string | null;
}
export function pickTarget(
  targets: readonly GridDropTarget[],
  clientX: number,
  clientY: number
): GridDropTarget | null; // pure hit test, React layer

// React: <GridTransferProvider> + useGridTransferMonitor(handlers) => cleanup
// GridLayout prop: onItemTransfer?(e) => void | false
```

**How the open PRs fit.**

- **PR #2260** (`CrossGridDragProvider`, open) touches `README.md`, `src/core/types.ts`, `src/react/components/GridLayout.tsx`, adds `src/react/context/CrossGridDrag*.ts(x)` and `useCrossGridDrag.ts`, and adds example 22 (`gh pr view 2260`). It overlaps the engine PRs on `GridLayout.tsx` and `core/types.ts`. Recommendation: do not merge it before the engine. Ask the author to rebase it onto `GridTransferProvider`, keeping its hit-testing and ghost rendering as the reference implementation, and reuse its tests and example (open question 4).
- **PR #2267** (`dragGroupIds`) targets the v1 `lib/*.jsx` tree, renames the package to `react-grid-layout-plus` and commits `dist/` builds (`gh pr view 2267`). It cannot merge. Its design ideas carry over: apply the same delta to every id in the group, and keep the original layering during the drag. Those become `GridTransferSession.group` and an engine rule. Recommendation: close it with a pointer to this RFC.
- **Test.** Pure `pickTarget` tests. A two-grid RTL test in which grid A ends its interaction with `cancel` and grid B's engine receives `start{kind:"drop"}`, `propose` and `stop`. A Playwright scenario with two grids.

### 14. Injectable store and public gesture interfaces (Later)

```ts
// GridLayout props (later): store?: GridStore
//   plus drag, resize and drop interfaces, introduced with a second
//   implementation (the pointer-events experiment, §8)
```

- **Demand.** None found for the store (pluggability §2). It is listed because external undo/redo and devtools would use it, and because the internal store makes it nearly free.
- **Why later.** A public store freezes `GridEngineState` and `GridAction` as public API. That should wait until two releases have used them internally. The same holds for the gesture interfaces, which wait for a second implementation.
- **Test.** The store contract is pure: dispatch, then the snapshot changes and subscribers are called once.

---

## 5. Performance Plan

### Measured baselines

**Core, per operation** (rgl-core §3.1: Node 24, Apple Silicon, `bench.cjs`). The generator mixes sizes from 1x1 to 3x3 with +-1 row jitter, then pre-compacts. A "drag frame" moves item 0 to the middle row. Treat the ms values as orders of magnitude. The n=50 row includes JIT warmup noise.

| n    | `moveElement` only | drag frame (move + `verticalCompactor`) | drag frame (move + `fastVerticalCompactor`) | `verticalCompactor.compact` | `horizontalCompactor.compact` |
| ---- | ------------------ | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- | ----------------------------- |
| 50   | 0.07 ms            | 1.2 ms                                  | 0.2 ms                                      | ~1 ms                       | 0.6 ms                        |
| 500  | **16 ms**          | **48 ms**                               | **22 ms**                                   | **51 ms**                   | 32 ms                         |
| 5000 | **8,480 ms**       | **20,100 ms**                           | **10,600 ms**                               | 3,300 ms                    | 3,390 ms                      |

These run about 10x above RFC 0001's baseline (vertical compaction at 500 items = 4.9 ms, move = about 7 us; `rfcs/0001:1494-1510`). That benchmark (`test/spec/benchmark-test.js:30-42`) uses a uniform 2x2 tiling, which never cascades. Mixed sizes trigger the recursive cascade. This RFC treats the mixed-size corpus as the realistic case.

**The columns do not add up, and are not meant to.** At n=500, `moveElement` only (16 ms) plus `verticalCompactor.compact` (51 ms) is 67 ms, but the drag frame is 48 ms. At n=5000, 8,480 + 3,300 = 11,780 ms, against a 20,100 ms frame. Each column is its own timing loop over different inputs:

- `verticalCompactor.compact` times `compact(base)` on the same uncloned, already-compacted `base` every repetition (`bench.cjs:13`). `compact` writes to its input in place (C-M1), but a check in this revision found `base` unchanged after one and two calls, so every repetition sees the same input.
- The drag frame times a fresh `cloneLayout(base)`, one `moveElement` and one `compact` of the moved layout (`bench.cjs:21`). "`moveElement` only" times the clone and the move without the compact (`bench.cjs:22`).
- Each loop runs one warmup call and then 20 repetitions at n=500, 3 at n=5000 (`bench.cjs:4,9`).

So the compact inside a drag frame runs on a different layout from the compact column. Why the totals differ by that much has not been measured. The millisecond columns stay informational. Targets are checked on op counts from the RGL-02 harness (#2323), which reproduces the count rows below exactly.

**Core, operation counts** (rgl-core §3.1, `count.cjs`):

| n    | `moveElement`, one drag frame                | `verticalCompactor`, already-compacted input                                |
| ---- | -------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 50   | 9 sorts, 9 filters                           | 46 `getStatics` filters, 50 `indexOf`, 45 `findIndex`                       |
| 500  | **180 sorts** (length sum 90k), 180 filters  | 496 filters (length sum 248k), 500 `indexOf` (250k), 495 `findIndex` (247k) |
| 2000 | **958 sorts** (length sum 1.9M), 958 filters | about 2000 of each, length sum about 4M each                                |

The figures in brackets are measured, but they are not elements scanned. `count.cjs` adds `this.length` on every call (`count.cjs:6`), so "250k" is 500 calls x a 500-item array. It is an upper bound for the scan. The scan itself is smaller. `layout.indexOf(sortedItem)` (`core/compactors.ts:200`) stops at the match, so on input whose order matches the sort order, finding item k takes k + 1 comparisons:

```
sum over k = 0..499 of (k + 1) = 500 x 501 / 2 = 125,250
```

That is a derived figure, under that ordering assumption. `findIndex` at `core/compactors.ts:50` has the same shape. RGL-10 (#2326) removes both calls, so their exact scan counts no longer matter.

**Where the O(n^2) terms come from:**

- `moveElement` re-sorts the full layout on every recursive call (`core/layout.ts:295`), copies it (`:325`) and scans all collisions (`:307`). That is O(k \* n log n) per mousemove, where k is the cascade depth (rgl-core §3.2, rank 1).
- Classic compactors call `resolveCompactionCollision` even on an already-compacted layout (495 of 500 items). Each call does a `findIndex` (`core/compactors.ts:50`) and a `getStatics` (`:53`), because `hasStatics` is not passed down from `:111` and `:149`. Restoring input order costs a `layout.indexOf` per item (`:200,239`; `extras/wrapCompactor.ts:87,118`) (rgl-core §3.2, ranks 2-3).
- `correctBounds` runs a static-collision loop that is O(dy \* n) per static (`core/layout.ts:230-232`). With S statics that overlap at the same cell and height h, that is about h x S^3 / 2 `collides` calls. The round-2 skeptic review measured 7, 24, 230 and 2,107 ms at S = 10, 20, 40 and 80 with h = 10,000. RGL-49 bounds it (§14).
- **The `collides` floor.** `compactItemVertical` moves each item up while `getFirstCollision(compareWith, l)` finds nothing, then resolves collisions until it finds nothing (`core/compactors.ts:104,110`). `getFirstCollision` scans the whole list when there is no collision (`core/collision.ts:44-50`), and `compareWith` grows by one item per placed item. So the final no-collision check alone costs at least:

  ```
  sum over k = 0..n-1 of k = n(n-1)/2
  n = 500:   500 x 499 / 2   =   124,750 collides calls
  n = 2000: 2000 x 1999 / 2  = 1,999,000 collides calls
  ```

  This is a lower bound from the code. #2323 measured 497,439 `collides` calls for one compaction at n=500 (`compact/mixed`). T1-T3 do not remove it (T14).

**React layer, per drag move** (rgl-react §2):

- `GridLayout` re-renders on every move, because `setActiveDrag(newObject)` and `setLayout(newArray)` always produce new references (`GridLayout.tsx:587-588`), even when the cell did not change.
- Memo defeats. An inline `gridConfig={{margin:[10,10]}}` re-renders every item. So do the legacy wrappers' destructured default `margin = [10,10]` (`legacy/ReactGridLayout.tsx:129`) and the v2 Responsive default (`ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:212`): on an identical rerender, every item re-rendered (probe LG). `getCompactor(type, overlap, true)` returns a new object per call (`core/compactors.ts:349-351`). `createScaledStrategy(scale)` runs per legacy render (`legacy/ReactGridLayout.tsx:228`).
- Synchronous layout reads per move:
  - `offsetParent.clientHeight` when `isBounded` (`GridItem.tsx:539-540`)
  - the same read on every resize event (`GridItem.tsx:695`)
  - edge scroll `getBoundingClientRect` per rAF (`edgeScroll.ts:56`)
  - drop `getBoundingClientRect` per dragover (`dropMath.ts:78`)
- `processGridItem` is a `useCallback` with 27 dependencies, recreated every render (`GridLayout.tsx:1169-1269`).
- `deepEqual` compares `layout` at least 4 times after a drag stop (rgl-react §2.5).
- `GridBackground` renders rows x cols `<rect>` nodes (`extras/GridBackground.tsx:132-150`; 12 x 100 = 1200). Its default `margin=[10,10]` breaks its three `useMemo` dependency lists every render (rgl-core §3.2, rank 8).

**react-draggable, per move** (draggable §3):

- 1 `offsetParent` read per move (measured in jsdom). In a browser with a positioned parent, add `offsetParent.getBoundingClientRect()` plus `scrollLeft`/`scrollTop` (`domFns.ts:129-133`). RGL's grid is positioned, so RGL pays this on every move.
- `findDOMNode()` twice per move (`positionFns.ts:81,90`).
- `Draggable` with `bounds="parent"`: 6 `getComputedStyle` calls per move (measured). RGL uses `DraggableCore` only, so this does not reach RGL.

**react-resizable, per move** (resizable §3):

- 1 forced `getBoundingClientRect` on the handle per event (`Resizable.tsx:103`), including `se`/`e`/`s` handles and the stop event, where it is not needed. 7 calls for 5 moves.
- 10 moves gave 10 `Resizable` renders, **80 `DraggableCore` renders and 240 new closures** with 8 handles. `render` calls `this.resizeHandler(...)` three times per handle (`Resizable.tsx:220-222`).
- RGL passes a fresh `draggableOpts={{disabled}}` and fresh `minConstraints`/`maxConstraints` arrays on every render (`GridItem.tsx:900,859-866`).

### Targets

Targets are goals for this RFC, not measurements. Op counts are deterministic and get budgets. Milliseconds are informational.

| T2  | Sorts per drag frame, n=2000                                                          | 958                                                               | 1                                                            | same; #2327 measured 958 to 1                                                                                                                                               |
| T3  | `indexOf` + `findIndex` + `getStatics` filters per `verticalCompactor.compact`, n=500 | 500 + 495 + 496 calls (#2323)                                     | 0 + 0 + 1                                                    | index map, carried indices, `hasStatics` once: RGL-10 (#2326, measured 0 + 0 + 1)                                                                                           |
| T4  | Drag frame (move + `verticalCompactor`), n=500                                        | 48 ms (rgl-core; informational)                                   | under 16 ms (`rfcs/0001:1527` budget)                        | T1-T3 and T14; not reachable through T1-T3 alone if T14 dominates; **to measure**                                                                                           |
| T5  | Drag frame (move + `fastVerticalCompactor`), n=500                                    | 22 ms                                                             | under 8 ms                                                   | T1-T2; **to measure** after                                                                                                                                                 |

| T9  | Layout reads per drag move in RGL (`isBounded`)                                       | 1 `clientHeight` (`GridItem.tsx:539-540`)                         | 0 per move; measured once at start                           | `containerHeight` on the `start` action (RGL-30)                                                                                                                            |

| T11 | `getBoundingClientRect` per RR resize event, `se` handle                              | 1                                                                 | 0 (`n`/`w` handles keep 1)                                   | RR gated read                                                                                                                                                               |

| T14 | `collides` calls per `verticalCompactor.compact`, already-compacted input, n=500      | 497,439 measured (#2323); derived floor 124,750                   | budget from the count measured after RGL-49                  | RGL-49: one pass per item instead of one scan per row step. RGL-10 keeps `collides` unchanged on purpose (#2326). A row-band index that removes the n(n-1)/2 floor is Later |

### Benchmark harness

The op-count harness is RGL-02, merged as #2323. Its design, from that PR:

- **Location.** `test/bench/opcounts.bench-test.ts`, run by `yarn bench:ops` through `test/jest.ops.cjs`. A Babel plugin loaded only by that config (`test/bench/instrument.cjs`) adds entry probes, so no file under `src/` changes. It shares `test/util/setupTests.js`. The timsort patch (`setupTests.js:7`) changes tie order, and op counts depend on tie order, so benchmarks and golden masters run under the same patch (rgl-tests §6a). Wall-clock timing stays in the opt-in `yarn bench` (`test/jest.bench.cjs`).
- **Corpus.** Seed 42 and the golden generators (`test/golden/generators.ts`), 12 columns, n in {50, 500, 2000}. Scenarios: `move/mixed`, `drag/mixed`, `compact/mixed`, `drag-10/packed`, `drag-subcell-10/packed` (T7), `resize/sparse` and `parent-rerender/packed` (T8). Setup and cleanup are not counted.
- **Counters.** `sort`, `filter`, `indexOf` and `findIndex` calls on layout arrays; entries into `collides`, `getFirstCollision`, `cloneLayoutItem`, `getStatics` and `compact`; DOM reads of `getBoundingClientRect`, `offsetParent`, `clientHeight` and `getComputedStyle`; `GridLayout` and `GridItem` renders and per-item Profiler commits.
- **Output.** `test/bench/baseline.json`, committed. `yarn bench:ops:update` rewrites it.
- **Gating.** Decided: a decrease passes; any increase, or a missing scenario or counter, fails with the delta. A PR that raises a count says why. CI runs it as its own step after the unit tests (about 50 s), outside `yarn test` and the pre-commit hook. Milliseconds never gate.
- **Work bounds.** The §14 budget scenarios (`test/bench/security-scenarios.ts`, #2328) live in the same suite: absolute per-probe caps, fewer than 50 n^2 `collides` calls, and baseline gating. The wrap pair at heights 10^4 and 10^9 must record equal counts: the work may not depend on the size of a number in the input.
- **What it reproduced.** #2323 reproduces the audit's counts exactly: 9/180/958 sorts for `moveElement` at n=50/500/2000, and 500 `indexOf`, 495 `findIndex` and 496 `getStatics` calls for one compaction at n=500.
- **Removing timing from Jest.** The seven wall-clock assertions in `test/spec/benchmark-test.js` (`:172,180,198,213,227,258`, and the drag check at `:293`) failed on a loaded machine: 3 failed under coverage, and 1 failed alone (horizontal 1000 items took 794 ms against a 500 ms limit; rgl-tests §4). They, the relative-timing blocks in `fast-compactor-test.js` and `fast-horizontal-compactor-test.js`, and the two determinism snapshots run only under the opt-in `yarn bench` (done in #2320).
- **RD and RR.** Each repo gets the same kind of counter suite with a committed baseline for T10-T12, in the PRs that claim them (RD-02, RR-02).

---

## 6. Clarity Plan

### Rules

These rules apply to every file this RFC touches. Reviewers cite the rule number.

1. **One job per function.** `moveElement` today writes the position, sorts, queries collisions, applies the overlap and prevent policies and orchestrates the cascade through mutual recursion (rgl-core §4.3). After the split: `resolveDrag` orchestrates, `collisionPolicy` decides push vs swap vs probe, `cascade` moves colliders. `correctBounds` splits into three passes (horizontal fix, Infinity fix, static de-overlap) behind the same exported function.
2. **Axis abstraction for vertical and horizontal mirrors.** One descriptor:

   ```ts
   interface Axis {
     readonly pos: "x" | "y";
     readonly size: "w" | "h";
     readonly cross: "x" | "y";
     readonly crossSize: "w" | "h";
     compare(a: LayoutItem, b: LayoutItem): number;
   }
   ```

   `compactItemVertical`/`compactItemHorizontal` (`core/compactors.ts:90-164`) and `verticalCompactor`/`horizontalCompactor` (`:178-246`) become one classic compactor in `core/compaction/classic.ts`, driven by descriptors in `core/geometry/axis.ts` (RGL-10, #2326). The built-in compactor objects stay exported. The mutating per-item helper exports are removed in 3.0 (§12).

3. **No `any`, no `as unknown as`, no casts that hide a type error.** Today `GridItem.tsx` has 9 `e as unknown as MouseEvent`/`Event` casts (`:405, 440, 468, 490, 522, 566, 629, 812, 831`). They hide RX-7. Other casts: `resizeHandle as ResizeHandle` (`GridLayout.tsx:1191`), `margin as [number, number]` (4 places), `rest as unknown as P` (`WidthProvider.tsx:129`), `Mutable<LayoutItem>` writes in `onResize` (`GridLayout.tsx:709-716,765`) (rgl-react §3.3). One `getClientPoint(e: MouseEvent | TouchEvent)` replaces the event casts. ESLint enforces `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` and `no-unsafe-*` on `src/`.
4. **Guard clauses over nesting.** `compactHorizontalFast` has a 95-line loop body with 4 nesting levels (`extras/fastHorizontalCompactor.ts:154-227`). The cyclomatic-complexity lint already in the repo gets a per-function budget for `src/core`.
5. **Lookup tables over string lists and switch chains.** Resize handle behavior is spread over string lists in `GridLayout.tsx:674-685`, `:714` and `:762`, and 8 handlers in `resizeItemInDirection` (`core/position.ts:239`). One table:

   ```ts
   const HANDLE: Record<
     ResizeHandleAxis,
     {
       movesX: boolean;
       movesY: boolean;
       growsW: 1 | 0 | -1;
       growsH: 1 | 0 | -1;
     }
   >;
   ```

6. **Core functions that return a result never mutate their inputs.** The policy covers every function whose output is its return value: each compactor's `compact`, `moveElement`, `correctBounds` and every new function. They stop mutating in 2.4.0 (RGL-11, RGL-14). Their outputs stay byte-identical; only the golden `inputAfter` column changes. One changelog line covers it (§12). Every internal caller of `correctBounds` already reads its return value (`GridLayout.tsx:282`, `ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:189`, `core/responsive.ts:149`, `react/hooks/useGridLayout.ts:169,199,356,390,429`), so that change needs no caller edits. The exported helpers that work by mutation, `resolveCompactionCollision` (returns `void`, `core/compactors.ts:38-44`, writes at `:75`) and `compactItemVertical`/`compactItemHorizontal`, keep their behavior throughout 2.4.x and are removed from public barrels in 3.0.0. The 2.4 JSDoc notice points to the 3.0 guide; no runtime warning promises an unavailable replacement. RGL-49 bounds the work of `compactItemVertical` and `compactItemHorizontal` without changing that contract (§14). A custom compactor built on them keeps working on 2.4.x; 3.0 authors use pure compactor operations or rewrite on `classicResolveDrag` where appropriate. The classic compactor uses a pure internal helper that returns a new layout instead. That makes the `layout.ts:5` header "All functions treat layouts as immutable" true for the functions it describes (rgl-core §4.2). There is no wrapper that replays writes onto the caller's objects.
7. **One ref-mirror helper.** Render-phase ref writes (`GridLayout.tsx:407,424,438`, `GridItem.tsx:273,646-647`) are unsafe under concurrent rendering. All of them go through `useLatest`, which assigns in a layout effect (rgl-react §2.3).
8. **Names say what the code does.** For example, `preventCollision` inside `moveElementAwayFromCollision` means "collider is static" (`core/layout.ts:385`). `isUserAction` is reused as a "try once" flag (`:389`). `movingUp` is true for horizontal moves (`:296-301`) (rgl-core §4.2).
9. **Comments match the code.** Each wrong comment in rgl-core §4.2 and rgl-react §3.3 is fixed in the PR that touches its file. Examples: `GridItem.tsx:691-694` ("offsetParent is the grid container", false), `edgeScroll.ts:8-9` ("or the window", false), `extras/fastHorizontalCompactor.ts:83` (sort order), and the unreachable branch at `:205-208`.
10. **Core types are React-free.** No `React.*` in `src/core` (`core/types.ts:329,533-537`; `core/position.ts:271-314`). `calcStyle` returns `csstype`'s `Properties<string | number>`, which `React.CSSProperties` extends, so existing strategies stay assignable (§3). A lint rule bans `react` imports under `src/core`.
11. **2.4 preserves default types; 3.0 makes them readonly and freezes the shared values.** The mutable defaults are `defaultConstraints` (`core/constraints.ts:381`), `default*Config` (`core/types.ts:468-592`) and `DEFAULT_BREAKPOINTS`/`DEFAULT_COLS` (`useResponsiveLayout.ts:34-49`). In 2.4, widen constraint sinks to `readonly LayoutConstraint[]` (`core/constraints.ts:400-401,447-448`). In 3.0, freeze each default and its owned nested arrays/objects, and make `LayoutItem` fields readonly. Consumer `data` is opaque and never frozen. Legacy uses mutable copies and mutable v1 type aliases. Its current non-bounded branch shares `defaultConstraints` (`legacy/ReactGridLayout.tsx:238-240`), so absorption must fix both branches.

12. **Freeze inputs only in tests.** The golden and reducer suites deep-freeze inputs with `test/util/deepFreeze.js`. Consumer layouts are never frozen at runtime. Exported default constants are the explicit exception from 3.0 onward, frozen in both development and production (rule 11).

## 7. Bug Triage

### ID scheme and dispositions

The audits reuse labels such as "B1" and "R3", so IDs here carry a repo prefix:

| Prefix | Source           | Severity source                               |
| ------ | ---------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| `C-`   | rgl-core §0, §5  | audit                                         |
| `RX-`  | rgl-react §0, §4 | audit; RFC-assigned where the audit gave none |
| `D-`   | draggable §5     | audit                                         |
| `Z-`   | resizable §5     | **RFC-assigned** (the audit gives none)       |

Duplicates are merged. C-B2 and rgl-react §4.5 describe the same mutation, so they are one row. rgl-react §1.7's Responsive compactor-swap double fire is folded into RX-5.

Dispositions:

- **Pre-golden fix.** Fixed in its own PR before that layer's golden master is recorded. Only for user-visible regressions where a released version that worked can be named.
- **Golden diff (PR).** Pinned as `knownBug` first, then fixed in the named PR from §9, which carries the fixture diff.
- **Defer (issue).** Pinned as `knownBug`, with an issue opened. Not fixed in 2.4.0 or 3.0.0.
- **Pin.** Current behavior is v1 behavior, or fixing it would break a contract in a minor. Kept, and documented as intended.

"Breaking if fixed" means a consumer could observe the fix through a public API (callback payloads, call counts, output order, exported function results).

### Totals

79 rows, unchanged by revision 5. Counted over the table:

- 2 pre-golden fixes: RX-1 and RX-2.
- 63 fixed in named PRs: 25 in 2.4.0 RGL PRs + 22 in 3.0.0 RGL PRs + 16 upstream = 63. C-B3e remains pinned within its otherwise fixed row.
- 8 deferred: 6 with an issue (C-B8, C-B15, RX-11d, Z-9, Z-10, D-S2), plus D-R11 and Z-12.
- 4 pinned rows: RX-15a, D-ctrl, Z-5 and Z-8.
- 2 others: Z-13 deprecated, D-R1 fixed only in the harness.

Arithmetic: 2 + 63 + 8 + 4 + 2 = 79. Revision 4 had 58 fixed, 9 deferred and 8 pinned. C-B9, C-B10, RX-14 and RX-17 move from pinned to 3.0 fixes; C-B13 moves from deferred to 3.0 removal: 58 + 4 + 1 = 63 fixed, 9 - 1 = 8 deferred, 8 - 4 = 4 pinned. RX-11a already counted as a fix and now resolves the entire drop callback contract in 3.0, so it adds no row. RX-14's warning remains in 2.4 and C-B13's factory warning remains there too.

## 8. Dependency Plan

### Where things stand

|                                    | react-draggable                       | react-resizable                                   |
| ---------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| RGL declared range                 | `^4.4.6` (package.json:111)           | `^3.2.0` (package.json:112)                       |
| RGL lockfile (dev and CI)          | 4.5.0 (yarn.lock:9269-9271)           | 3.2.0 (yarn.lock:9419-9421)                       |
| Latest release                     | 4.7.2 (RD package.json)               | 4.0.2 (RR package.json)                           |
| What a fresh consumer install gets | **4.7.2**, because `^4.4.6` allows it | 3.2.0, because `^3.2.0` excludes 4.x              |
| Its own dependency on RD           |                                       | `^4.5.0` in both 3.2.0 (yarn.lock:9424) and 4.0.2 |

The lockfile pin is not the consumer pin. RGL's own tests and CI run RD 4.5.0. A consumer installing RGL today can resolve RD 4.7.2. So two things are true at once:

1. **The `^4.4.6` floor is wrong.** `allowMobileScroll` landed in 4.5.0 (`node_modules/react-draggable/CHANGELOG.md:3-10`), so a consumer pinned to 4.4.6 silently loses it (RX-18).
2. **The 4.6.0 ctrl+click change is probably already reaching RGL users.** Since 4.6.0, ctrl+left-drag no longer starts a drag on any OS (`DraggableCore.tsx:322`, commit `fb246a2`; draggable header table). RGL's tests have never run against it. Raising the floor does not decide this behavior. It needs its own line in the changelog and migration table (D-ctrl, open question 1).

What changed in RD between 4.5.0 and 4.7.2 (draggable header table): the ctrl+click change; `findDOMNode` no longer falls back when `nodeRef.current` is null (RGL always passes `nodeRef`); the TypeScript rewrite with ESM and an `exports` map; a `nonce` prop; type and bundler fixes.

What changed in RR from 3.2.0 to 4.0.2 (RR CHANGELOG): 4.0.0 is a types-only major (bundled TypeScript declarations, Flow shims removed). 4.0.1 changes `lockAspectRatio` to orthogonal projection. RGL does not use `lockAspectRatio` or `axis` (rgl-react §6.2). So moving to RR 4 should not change RGL behavior. The RGL contract tests and traces confirm this before the bump merges.

### Changes per repo

**RGL**

One path per dependency in 2.4.0 and 3.0.0. No feature detection.

- RGL-08 (2.4.0): `react-draggable: ^4.5.0`. `react-resizable: ^4.0.2`. React peer `>= 16.8.0`. Delete `src/react/types/react-resizable.d.ts` and the `handle` cast at `GridItem.tsx:893-896`, using RR's bundled types instead.
- RGL always uses its own `getClientPoint`, so the touch fix (RX-7) works on RD 4.5.0 and needs no new RD fields.
- The RR floor stays `^4.0.2` until a feature RGL cannot do without needs a higher one, and then the floor moves, with no fallback path.
- RGL keeps passing `minConstraints`/`maxConstraints` to RR, so the live pixel box during resize stays clamped (§6).
- CI matrix (RGL-07): RD {4.5.0 floor, latest 4.x} x React {18.3, 19.x}, all with RR 4.0.2 and on the one Node version in `.nvmrc` (RGL-45). That is 4 cells. The RR axis {4.0.2 floor, latest 4.x} joins in the PR that gates RR 4.1.0, when it first has two values, which makes 8. The contract tests assert structural subsets, so additive upstream fields pass in both floor and latest cells. React 16.8 and 17 smoke tests run only on the 2.4.x line (RGL-07); 3.0 requires >=18 with no shim (§11).
- Upstream releases reach RGL users through the caret range without an RGL release. So RD and RR publish to `next` first, and a CI job installs the candidate into RGL master and the 2.4.x line before `latest` moves (§15).

**react-draggable, 4.8.0 then 4.9.0 (minors)**

- RD-01: Yarn 4 through Corepack. At the audit HEAD the repo pinned `yarn@1.22.22` and CI used `--frozen-lockfile` (draggable §2). Merged as [RD #837](https://github.com/react-grid-layout/react-draggable/pull/837) (`7639cd1`), with a lint-target follow-up in [RD #839](https://github.com/react-grid-layout/react-draggable/pull/839) (`f787bc3`).
- RD-02 (its own release, 4.9.0, after 4.8.0): cache the `offsetParent` rect and scroll at drag start, invalidated on capture-phase `scroll` and on `resize`. One `findDOMNode` per event. Drop per-move `findInArray` closures (draggable §3; T10). A separate minor, so a stale cache in a scrolled or transformed parent can be reverted without reverting the fixes.
- RD-03: a truthful handler type. `DraggableEventHandler` declares `e: MouseEvent` (`lib/utils/types.ts:3`), but touch handlers receive a `TouchEvent`; the handler takes the `DraggableEvent` union instead (`:30-34`; draggable §4 typing holes). No new `DraggableData` fields: RGL computes client points itself, and no RD issue asks for them (D-S2 is deferred).
- RD-04: per-instance listener pair instead of the module-global `dragEventFor`, a `dragging` guard on start and move, and a touch-safe cancel path. Fixes D-R3, D-R4, D-R10, D-R2.
- RD-05: idle unmount stops clearing the selection (D-R5). Rebind touchstart when the node changes (D-R9). `Draggable` applies bounds to `onStop` and commits x/y at stop (D-R6, D-R7).
- RD-06: delete dead code (IE paths at `domFns.ts:61-66,80-85,215-220`, the `initMouseEvent` fallback at `DraggableCore.tsx:407-413`, the dead warning D-R8, Flow comments). Fix the vacuous jsdom tests by dispatching each event in its own `act()` (D-R1).

**react-resizable, 4.1.0 (minor)**

- RR-01: Yarn 4 through Corepack. At the audit HEAD `yarn test` was broken under Corepack: there was no `packageManager` field and the lockfile was Yarn 1. Merged as [RR #280](https://github.com/react-grid-layout/react-resizable/pull/280) (`1b40303`). Reorder CI so the built-artifact suite runs after `yarn build` (`test.yml:34-37`; resizable §2).
- RR-02: stable per-axis handlers cached like `handleRefs`; `getBoundingClientRect` only for `n`/`w` handles and never on stop; `shouldComponentUpdate` (resizable §3; T11, T12).
- RR-03: bug fixes only. Merge `draggableOpts` handlers, running the user's first and honoring `false` (Z-6); a dev warning for a function handle without a ref (Z-7); compose user refs (Z-11); deprecate `handleSize` (Z-13). No `element` or `positionCompensation`: RGL does not read them, and no RR issue asks for them.
- RR-04: capture the aspect ratio at start and re-project after the clamp; lock respects `axis`; NaN guards; synchronous `ResizableBox.onResize`. Fixes Z-1 to Z-4. Open [RR #278](https://github.com/react-grid-layout/react-resizable/pull/278) ("preserve locked aspect ratio within size constraints") targets Z-1; RR-04 reviews it first.

### Should RGL drop either dependency for an internal pointer layer?

| Factor                             | Keep both, behind translation functions                                                                                                                                                  | Replace with an internal pointer layer                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Bytes RGL ships for them           | RD `DraggableCore` + utils: **10,380 B gzip** (CJS, unminified, installed 4.5.0). RR `Resizable` + utils: **4,689 B gzip** (unminified, installed 3.2.0). Minified sizes **to measure**. | Saves those bytes, minus the new layer (**to measure**). RGL's own ESM output measured 23,625 B gzip for all `dist/*.mjs` concatenated, but that build is stale (built 12:08 on Oct 5, HEAD committed 13:44), so it is **to measure**. |
| Code to own                        | Translation functions only                                                                                                                                                               | Today's equivalent at the dep HEADs: `DraggableCore.tsx` 508 lines + utils 503 lines (RD 4.7.2), `Resizable.tsx` 231 lines (RR 4.0.2). Note these are HEAD counts, not the installed 4.5.0 and 3.2.0.                                  |
| Behavior already pinned by tests   | RD: 204 unit + 23 browser tests. RR: 83 tests (draggable §2, resizable §2). Plus the new golden traces.                                                                                  | Rebuild that coverage: iframe realm checks, shadow-DOM-aware handle matching (`domFns.ts:39-48`), touch identifier filtering, the user-select hack, `allowMobileScroll`.                                                               |
| Per-move layout reads              | RD: 1 `offsetParent` read plus a rect and 2 scroll reads per move in a browser; RR: 1 rect per event. Both fixable upstream (T10, T11).                                                  | 0 per move by design: RGL already knows the grid rect.                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Known bugs reaching RGL            | D-R3, D-R4, D-R5, D-R9, D-R10. All fixable upstream by the same maintainer.                                                                                                              | Gone, but a new layer brings its own.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Features missing in both           | Pointer Events and pointer capture (D-R11), cancel reasons                                                                                                                               | Native                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Effect on other users of RD and RR | Upstream fixes help everyone                                                                                                                                                             | None; RD and RR lose their main driver of fixes                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Workarounds RGL carries            | Removed by the translation functions: cancel prefix, fake `DraggableData`, event casts                                                                                                   | Removed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

**Recommendation: keep both dependencies in 2.x, behind internal translation functions.**

- Every RGL-visible defect in them is fixable upstream in a minor, by the same maintainer.
- The byte savings are about 15 KB gzip unminified, and the minified figure has not been measured.
- The 3.0.0 translation functions remove RGL's workarounds either way.
- After 3.0.0, build a `pointerEventsAdapter` as an opt-in experiment (L5). It is the second implementation that justifies drag and resize interfaces, and it gives `setPointerCapture` and `lostpointercapture` as a cancel reason.
- Decide on an internal 3.x change or a later major using three measurements: golden-trace parity across the scenario suite, the measured minified byte delta, and layout reads per move.

## 9. Phased Delivery

### Sizes and conventions

- **S** is under about 200 changed lines, **M** about 200-600, **L** over 600. Generated golden fixtures are not counted.
- IDs are stable labels, not merge order. Revision 2 merged RGL-16 into RGL-15 and RGL-33 into RGL-32, and added RGL-43 to RGL-47. Revision 3 folded RGL-44 into RGL-23 and added RGL-48 and RGL-49. Revision 4 split RGL-11a (the sort-once half, merged as #2327) out of RGL-11 and folded RGL-42 into RGL-23, RGL-24 and RGL-25; nothing was renumbered. "Depends on" gives the order.
- "Byte-identical" names the golden suites that must not change. The golden suites already fail on any changed or missing fixture in an ordinary run; only `GOLDEN_UPDATE=1` writes them (`test/golden-react/README.md` on master). "Expected diff" names the fixtures that may change. The PR description explains each changed fixture, and a fix removes the fixture's `knownBug` tag.
- Every PR runs the full Jest suite, lint, `yarn fmt:check`, `yarn bench:ops` (from RGL-02), the Playwright smoke tests and `test/e2e/live-interaction.spec.ts` (required from RGL-07 on). Those are not repeated per row.
- Every PR with an "Expected diff" adds a `CHANGELOG.md` entry. The PR template checklist asks for it (RGL-45); review checks it.

### Releases

| Release | Phases                                                            | Contents                                                                                       | Ships                                                              |
| ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 2.4.0   | 1 (tests, CI), 2 (upstream, gated), 3 (core refactors), 4 (fixes) | No engine or extension point. Fixes, bounds, actionable deprecations and non-breaking hygiene. | `2.4.0-rc.N` on `next` for at least one week, then `latest` (§15)  |
| 3.0.0   | 5 (engine), 6 (React extraction, perf), 7 (extension points)      | The engine, §4 extension points, §12 breaks, migration guide and codemod                       | `3.0.0-rc.N` on `next` for at least two weeks, then `latest` (§15) |

### Non-breaking 2.4 hygiene

RGL-08 removes unused prop-types and resize-observer-polyfill (package.json:114,117; zero src imports; WidthProvider.tsx:81 uses native ResizeObserver). RGL-13 widens both constraint sinks to readonly arrays (core/constraints.ts:400-401,447-448). RGL-43 removes obsolete published root entry files (package.json:64-68; index.js:1-8 points into build/, absent from this checkout), aligns .browserslistrc:2 with tsconfig.json:4, and refactors WidthProvider without adding root or /react exports.

Removing fast-equals is non-breaking hygiene once all its uses have equivalent comparisons. The four imports are GridLayout.tsx:19, ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:15, useGridLayout.ts:9 and useResponsiveLayout.ts:9. The responsive component also compares breakpoints and cols (:332-336), so layout comparisons alone cannot remove it. Stage the cleanup in 2.4 only if a differential proves all existing equality behavior unchanged, including constraint objects. Otherwise finish it with RGL-50 after the 3.0 comparison contracts land. No equality semantics change is pulled into the minor merely to remove a dependency.

### Phase 5 (3.0.0): legacy absorption, then the engine

RGL-52 lands on the released 2.4 baseline before any modern binding break. It depends on RGL-21 and 2.4.0, not on RGL-24 or RGL-35. RGL-23 can build the unwired reducer in parallel. Every Phase 5-7 PR keeps the post-2.4 legacy traces byte-identical: callback order/payloads, controlled-layout and cancel behavior, data-grid re-seeding, responsive width arguments, DOM/class timing and normalized output keys. A modern expected diff never authorizes a legacy diff. If an absorber needs the new implementation to exist, its enabling change and compatibility path must land atomically in that breaking PR; they cannot be deferred to a later PR.

The engine PRs start from the 2.4.0 traces, which already carry the RX-5 and C-B2 fixes. There is no intermediate engine that reproduces today's mid-interaction behavior first. The queue and cancel rules land with the reducer (RGL-23) and the binding (RGL-24, RGL-25), the existing #2321 traces stay the baseline, and each PR lists the traces it changes as expected diffs. Those are the mid-interaction traces under `test/golden-react/__golden__/`: `controlled-layout-mid-drag.{v2,strict}`, `dragged-child-removed-mid-drag.v2`, `responsive-breakpoint-mid-drag.strict`, `internal-touch-cancel.touch` (`knownBug` `touch-cancel-no-stop`), `external-leave.vertical`, `external-touch-leave.vertical` and `external-touch-cancel.vertical`.

New golden: replaying every #2321 scenario through `reduceGrid` at grid-unit level equals the 2.4.0 React traces, except the mid-interaction traces above, each listed in the PR as an expected diff. Reducer tests pin the controlled-layout contract, each case resolving as latest wins: a parent one step stale at release gets its own layout with `RGL_W003`; a parent current at release gets the drag result with no warning; a parent current at release whose stop is vetoed by middleware ends at `startLayout`, and one whose stop is edited ends at the edit, both with no `RGL_W003`; a debounced update after stop wins; an intentional restore of an earlier layout mid-drag wins at release; a `minW` edit at unchanged geometry is pending and applies; a restore of an earlier `data` value is patched at once; a pending edit that carries new `data` applies its `data` at once and its geometry at release. Also a removal before the first `propose` that re-runs nothing, a controlled `onDrag` update plus an unrelated prop change during a drop hover that keeps the drop, a data-only update mid-drag, a rejected nudge with no interaction events and both permission override directions. Inputs deep-frozen in tests. The comparator differential (RX-19) passes, including the N-7 fixture. Second commit: C-B1, whose only expected diff is the C-B1 free-form scenario. Not exported from `/core`. | L    |

                      F1, F4 -> 03 (#2321, done) -> 04 ------.
                                |--> 05 -> 08 -----------+--> 07
                                `--> 06 -----------------'
                      45 (no deps; before 07);  46 (before 13)
