Remove only the explicitly listed exports.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| K7   | Keep CJS and ESM, Responsive flat props (`ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:41-78`), `innerRef` (`GridLayout.tsx:150`; add `ref` only additively), the internal engine/store boundary (§4 rank 14), no plugin registry, C-B3e output order, the three internal `collides` variants, `dragConfig.threshold`, `Mutable`/`DeepPartial`, and Compactor `allowOverlap`/`preventCollision` (`core/types.ts:274-286`). No concrete gain justifies changing these. |

### Other migration notes

2.4 retains the documented bug-fix and bounds migrations: read returned layouts instead of mutated inputs (RGL-11/RGL-14), expect one notification at stop (RX-5), normalize saved layouts at intake (§14), upgrade the declared dependency floors (§8), and use CSS for GridBackground's one-path DOM (RGL-34). Legacy Responsive returns to its v1 zero threshold (RX-13a). The RD ctrl+click behavior remains OQ1.

3.0 also ships the engine's controlled-layout and cancelled-stop contracts (§3), comparator exceptions (RX-19), commit-only hook notifications (RX-9), touch-drop `onTouchDragOver`, breakpoint cancellation through `interactionKey`, and corrected responsive margin/padding callbacks (RX-16). A sync-phase strategy throw now reports through `onError`/`console.error` and keeps the last good layout, so consumers must move error-boundary recovery to `onError`. RGL-53's guide covers every row here and every §7 behavior change.

The jscodeshift codemod (RGL-51) handles mechanical rows only, emits a report for manual cases, and is idempotent. Its fixtures compile against the built 3.0 package. The guide and legacy absorber are release gates, not follow-ups.

## 13. Open Questions

Unresolved items need a maintainer decision. OQ6/OQ7 are resolved by revision 5; OQ8 asks the panel to review the adopted wrapper default. Recommendations first. Items settled in rounds 1 and 2 moved to "Decisions already made" (§2), and the rest were renumbered.

1. **ctrl+click (RD 4.6.0+).** Accept the "ctrl+left-drag does not start a drag" behavior for all RGL users, or restore the old behavior with an RGL-side override?
   - _Recommendation_: accept it and document it, in both the RD 4.8.0 notes and the RGL 2.4.0 notes. Fresh installs already get it through `^4.4.6`. `allowAnyClick` would also enable right- and middle-button drags.
2. **A 2.3.1 patch for RX-2.** RX-2 is a High regression shipped in 2.3.0. RGL-01 is an S-sized fix with a known cause, and RX-1 is already on master (`1468c0f`). Cut 2.3.1 from a `release/2.3` branch with RX-1 and RGL-01, before Phase 3 starts?
   - _Recommendation_: yes. It is a publishing decision, so it is listed here rather than decided. Without it, both regressions stay in the shipped version until 2.4.0.
3. **Drop react-draggable and react-resizable for an internal pointer layer?**
   - _Recommendation_: keep both in 3.0. Fix them upstream, ship the translation functions, then build pointerEventsAdapter as an opt-in experiment. Decide on a later major or an internal 3.x change using measured parity, bytes and layout reads (§8, KEEP K5).
4. **PR #2260 (cross-grid drag).** Merge before the engine, or rebase it onto the transfer interface (§4, rank 13)?
   - _Recommendation_: rebase after RGL-32 and RGL-36, reusing its hit-testing, ghost, tests and example. Close PR #2267 with a pointer to this RFC, since it targets the v1 tree and renames the package.
5. **Legacy `Responsive` threshold (RX-13a).** Restore v1's 0 px, or keep 2.x's accidental 3 px?
   - _Recommendation_: restore 0. The legacy entry point promises v1 behavior, and legacy `RGL` already uses 0.
6. **Resolved: external-drop drag callbacks (RX-11a).** 3.0 fires no drag callbacks for the placeholder, on commit or cancel. Use dropConfig hover callbacks and onDrop. This makes modern start/stop pairing total. Legacy keeps the v1 sequence (BREAK 2).
7. **Resolved: `data-grid` on modern entries.** Warn once in 2.4 with RGL_W015, using explicit layout/layouts as the available replacement; remove in 3.0. Legacy owns construction and responsive re-seeding (BREAK 6). The current read is GridLayout.tsx:247-266; README:66 already describes the intended split.
8. **Panel review: wrapper div by default (BREAK 12).** Adopted for 3.0, but this is the lowest-confidence BREAK. Does ref ownership and removal of the forwarding contract justify the extra DOM node and selector/height migration for existing modern consumers? The panel should review RGL-38's DOM goldens and migration examples. The adopted default stands unless a later maintainer ruling reverses it; legacy keeps cloning.

---

## 14. Security

RGL renders layouts that often come from storage, a server or another user (shared dashboards). This section covers untrusted layout input. The grep in round 1 found no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, `eval`, `new Function` or `innerHTML` in `src/`, and no new API takes strings into one. The keyboard live region writes with `textContent` (§4, rank 9).

**Threat.** A collaborator who controls a saved layout can freeze the tab of everyone who opens it. Value bounds alone do not stop this, because several loops do work proportional to the size of a number, not the number of items:

| Loop                                                | Where                                                                                               | Work today                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| --------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Wrap compactor marks static cells one by one        | `for (let dy = 0; dy < s.h; dy++)` (`extras/wrapCompactor.ts:70-74`)                                | `h x w` `Set` entries per static: a static with `h: 1e9` runs a billion iterations                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Wrap compactor finds the next free cell             | `while (staticPositions.has(nextPos)) nextPos++` (`extras/wrapCompactor.ts:94`)                     | one step per blocked cell. With `cols: 1`, a static at `y: 0, h: 1e9` and one 1x1 item, the first legal row is 1e9 (executor, round 2)                                                                                                                                 |
| `correctBounds` moves overlapping statics down      | `while (getFirstCollision(collidesWith, l)) l.y++` (`core/layout.ts:230-232`)                       | one full scan per row: about h x S^3 / 2 `collides` calls for S statics; 2,107 ms at S = 80, h = 10,000 (skeptic, round 2). A jump past each collider still leaves about n^3 work in the item count: 1,000 overlapping statics took 558 ms on the #2328 head (round 3) |
| `correctBounds` places `y: Infinity` at the bottom  | `l.y = bottom(collidesWith)` (`core/layout.ts:223-225`)                                             | a static at `y: Infinity` keeps the bottom infinite, so the sentinel survives into compaction (pentester, round 3)                                                                                                                                                     |
| Classic compactors move each item toward the origin | `while (l.y > 0 && !getFirstCollision(compareWith, l)) l.y--` (`core/compactors.ts:104`)            | one full scan per row stepped; the start row is `min(bottom, y)` (`:101`), so one tall item makes it large (pentester, round 2). The exported `compactItemVertical` runs the same loop for direct callers                                                              |
| Fast horizontal compactor tracks a tide per row     | `new Array(maxRow).fill(0)` and a step per static row (`extras/fastHorizontalCompactor.ts:124,140`) | memory and work proportional to the tallest item: a direct call with a static `h: 1e9` allocates a billion entries (executor, round 3)                                                                                                                                 |

Non-finite values have a related failure: `collides` returns `true` for NaN (`core/collision.ts:24-27`), so "move until no collision" loops can spin (C-L1).

All of this ships in **2.4.0**: a bound on values at intake (RGL-48), and a bound on work in each loop (RGL-22, RGL-49).

### Intake bounds (RGL-48, 2.4.0)

The table describes 2.4 intake. From 3.0 the modern entry reads only explicit layouts and child ids; data-grid validation moves into the legacy adapter, including generated-breakpoint re-seeding.

RGL-48 is in review with RGL-49 as [#2328](https://github.com/react-grid-layout/react-grid-layout/pull/2328). One function, `validateLayoutInput(layout, { cols, maxRows })` in `core/validation.ts`, runs on every path a layout enters by. `react-grid-layout/core` exports it with `MAX_GRID_ROWS` for standalone use, and the README section "Bounds for saved layouts" documents it:

| Path                                                           | Call site (at `1468c0f`)                                                                                                                                    |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GridLayout` `layout` prop and `data-grid` children            | `synchronizeLayoutWithChildren`, before `correctBounds` (`GridLayout.tsx:282`)                                                                              |
| `ResponsiveGridLayout` `layouts` prop and `data-grid` children | its sync copy (`ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:189`), behind the ingest gate (`:274`)                                                                             |
| `ResponsiveGridLayout` initial layout                          | before `findOrGenerateResponsiveLayout` (`ResponsiveGridLayout.tsx:237-238`), which corrects and compacts a generated layout (`core/responsive.ts:149,155`) |
| `useGridLayout` initial layout and `setLayout`                 | `react/hooks/useGridLayout.ts:169,199`; the `setLayout` member (`:73`)                                                                                      |
| `useResponsiveLayout` `layouts`                                | behind its ingest gate (`react/hooks/useResponsiveLayout.ts:273`)                                                                                           |
| `useResponsiveLayout` setters                                  | `setLayoutForBreakpoint` and `setLayouts` (`react/hooks/useResponsiveLayout.ts:201,209`)                                                                    |
| Drop item                                                      | `droppingItem` and the `onDragOver` size override in `GridLayout`; `onDropDragOver` in the hook (`useGridLayout.ts:382-393`)                                |
| Legacy wrappers                                                | through the v2 paths above; they add no path of their own                                                                                                   |

Every responsive path validates each stored breakpoint layout with that breakpoint's `cols`, before any generation or compaction reads it. #2328 implements these paths.

**Trust boundary.** Validation runs only on external input: props, the hook setters, `data-grid` fields, the drop item, and stored responsive layouts when the component takes them in. It never runs on the grid's resolved state or on a direct core lookup. Collision resolution may legitimately place items beyond the input row limit, and re-validating resolved state would re-clamp them on every child or breakpoint change. #2328 removed five such resolved-state calls in `5a745531` and pins the rule with child-change and breakpoint-change tests.

In 3.0.0 the same function runs in the binding before `syncProps`, `setLayout` and `start{kind:"drop"}`, so the reducer never sees an unchecked layout.

**Rules.** These are the bounds #2328 ships (`core/validation.ts`, README). `rowLimit` is `MAX_GRID_ROWS` (10,000), lowered by a smaller finite `maxRows`, with a floor of 1. They are constants, not config.

| Field          | Bound                                                                                                                           |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `w`, `minW`    | `[1, cols]`                                                                                                                     |
| `x`            | `[0, cols - w]`                                                                                                                 |
| `h`, `minH`    | `[1, rowLimit]`                                                                                                                 |
| finite `y`     | `[0, rowLimit]`                                                                                                                 |
| `y: Infinity`  | kept as "place at bottom"; `correctBounds` resolves it against the bottom of the items whose `y` is finite (RGL-49, `dbd1cd7d`) |
| `maxW`, `maxH` | only raised to their minimum (`minW` or `minH`, else 1); larger values, `Infinity` included, pass unchanged                     |

- `NaN`, `-Infinity` and non-numbers take the lower bound. `+Infinity` takes the upper bound, except the `y` sentinel. Fractions are kept. Missing optional limits stay absent.
- `maxW` and `maxH` are not clamped to the grid (`9df78861`). `Infinity` is the documented default, a maximum above the grid causes no work because `w` and `h` are bounded on their own, and clamping would rewrite `Infinity` into every `onLayoutChange` payload and cap a later, wider grid.
- The minimums are bounded because `applySizeConstraints` clamps to them on every resize (`core/constraints.ts:95`). Without that, `minH: 1e9` would produce `h: 1e9` on the first resize, and `minH: NaN` would produce `h: NaN`.
- `x` is clamped into the current `cols`, so an item outside the grid moves inside at intake and `onLayoutChange` fires. That is a changelog item. `correctBounds`' pinned `x: 0, w: cols` rule for a negative `x` (C-B10) is now reached only by direct calls.
- Duplicate ids are kept, as today. #2328 does not check them; RGL-47 adds `RGL_W007` (C-B14).
- An unchanged layout is returned as the same object. A changed one emits one uncoded development `console.warn` per call, none in production. RGL-47, which owns the code registry and `docs/warnings.md`, assigns it `RGL_W002`; #2328 itself emits no code.
- These are intake bounds, not a cap on the final bottom: collision resolution can stack items below `rowLimit`.

In-bounds behavior is byte-identical. Out-of-bounds goldens change, as #2328's commits list: `cols-no-resync.{v2,legacy}`, the four `hook-cols-and-compactor-sync.{normal,strict}-{component,disagreements}` traces, and the core `extras-wrap/known-bug-e-static-infinity-budget`. `responsive/existing` changed in `c678f73d` and was restored in `5a745531`, once validation stopped running on resolved state.

### Work bounds (RGL-22 and RGL-49, 2.4.0, in #2328)

Exported core functions can be called directly with any input, so each loop is bounded on its own, not only through intake.

| Loop                                                          | Fix                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Output                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Fast horizontal tide (RGL-22, shipped in #2328 as `dc8d1830`) | The tide is stored as sparse sampled row spans, so a tall static costs a few spans instead of one entry per row (C-W4). An extent that is not an integer below 2^32 still throws the existing `RangeError`, without allocating.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Existing fast-horizontal tests and goldens unchanged. Measured: `fast-horizontal-tall` and `fast-horizontal-blocked` (static `h: 1e9`) make 0 `collides` calls and 6 row spans each.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Wrap occupancy (RGL-22)                                       | Occupied space is a list of rectangles per column band, with arithmetic overlap tests. A blocked candidate jumps past the blocking rectangle: to its right edge within the row, or, when no column in the row fits, to the lowest bottom edge among the blockers. No cell is enumerated.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Fixes C-B5 (wide and tall items overlap), so it is an expected diff. The executor's `cols: 1` case places the 1x1 item at row 1e9, with no overlap.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `correctBounds` statics (RGL-49)                              | Resolve each static with one sorted sweep over its x-overlapping colliders instead of repeated first-collision jumps: O(n log n) per static, O(n^2 log n) in total. The bottom for `y: Infinity` is computed over finite `y` only (C-W3). No item-count cap, because a cap drops user data.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Core and React goldens unchanged. Measured: 1,000 overlapping statics with `h: 10,000` make 0 `collides` calls and 1,000 sorts, with 1,000,000 blocker-collection steps (the n^2 term); 80 statics make 6,400.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Classic move toward origin (RGL-49)                           | Compute the row the upward loop stops at in one pass over `compareWith`. The loop visits the lattice `start, start - 1, ...` and stops at the first row that is at most 0 or collides. Each item's colliding rows form an open interval, so the first lattice row inside it is arithmetic. Then the existing downward resolution runs. The exported `compactItemVertical` and `compactItemHorizontal` get the same stop row and keep their mutation contract, so direct callers and custom compactors are bounded too (C-W4). | Byte-identical by construction; the core and React goldens and the oracle differential are the check. A changed golden means the restatement has a bug. No step cap: a cap of 10^4 steps, each a full scan, would still allow about 10^4 x n^2 `collides` calls. Measured: `large-y` (one item `h: 1e9`, another `y: 1e9`) and the direct `compactItemVertical`/`compactItemHorizontal` calls at 1e9 make 2 `collides` calls each. |

**Budget tests.** #2328 adds `test/bench/security-scenarios.ts`, run by `security.bench-test.ts` in the op-count suite, with every count gated against `baseline.json` like any other scenario. Each scenario's probes abort past an absolute cap, so broken code fails in milliseconds instead of hanging: 1,000 for direct helpers, 20,000 for the mixed wrap and fast horizontal cases, 200,000 for the original vectors, and 20 n^2 for the 1,000-static sweep. Every scenario also asserts fewer than 50 n^2 `collides` calls. The scenarios are `large-y`, `minH-resize`, `overlapping-statics` (n = 80), `overlapping-statics-1000`, `wrap-static`, `wrap-fractional`, `wrap-mixed-10000`, `wrap-mixed-billion`, `wrap-mixed-decimal`, `helper-large-y`, `helper-large-x`, `fast-horizontal-tall` and `fast-horizontal-blocked`. `wrap-mixed-10000` and `wrap-mixed-billion` run the same layout at heights 10^4 and 10^9 and record identical counts. `correct-bounds-sweep-test.ts` and `wrap-bounds-test.ts` cover the sweep and the wrap occupancy, and `layout-input-test.tsx` covers intake on every path.

**Strategies are trusted.** Compactors, constraints and middleware are code the consumer chose to run. The engine validates their outputs to keep its own invariants (§4), not to sandbox them.

---

## 15. Release

### Releases and channels

| Release | Contents                                                                                                         | Channel                                                                                                            |
| ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 2.3.1   | RX-1 and RX-2, if approved (open question 2)                                                                     | `latest`, from `release/2.3`                                                                                       |
| 2.4.0   | Phases 1-4: tests, CI, upstream floors, input and work bounds, core refactors, fixes as golden diffs. No engine. | `2.4.0-rc.N` on `next` for at least one week, then latest; maintained patches from release/2.4 on the 2.x dist-tag |
| 3.0.0   | Phases 5-7: engine, extraction, extension points, §12 breaks, guide and codemod                                  | `3.0.0-rc.N` on `next` for at least two weeks, then `latest`                                                       |

The release workflow already supports this. It is a `workflow_dispatch` with `prerelease` and `preminor` among its `semver` options and an npm `tag` input that defaults to `latest` (`.github/workflows/release.yml:4-25`). A 2.4 rc uses preminor then prerelease; a 3.0 rc uses premajor then prerelease, each with tag: next. The workflow already lists premajor and major (.github/workflows/release.yml:15-19). Promotion uses the matching minor/major release action on the tested rc commit.

There is no dual controller. The engine replaces the old one in 3.0.0. The rc windows and the per-frame pixel checks (RGL-06) are the guard instead. During each rc, the maintainer asks two or three large consumers to run it.

Splitting the releases keeps the Phase 4 fixes revertible on their own: they ship in 2.4.0 before any engine code exists, and each is one PR.

### Promote and abort criteria

The same form for both releases. A clock restarts when a new rc is cut.

|                        | 2.4.0                                                                                                                                                                                                  | 3.0.0                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Minimum time on `next` | 1 week from the last rc                                                                                                                                                                                | 2 weeks from the last rc                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Promote when all hold  | No open issue labeled `2.4-rc` (regressions reported against the rc). The CI matrix is green on the release commit. The Chromium per-frame suite is green. At least one named consumer reported a run. | No open issue labeled `3.0-rc`. The CI matrix is green. The per-frame suite is green in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. The manual touch-delay check passed on iOS Safari and Android Chrome (§4 rank 8). At least one consumer reported a run. The migration guide, codemod fixtures, legacy absorber and consumer-3.0 fixture set are green; every API diff maps to §12. |
| A regression in the rc | Fixed forward in the next rc, which restarts the clock                                                                                                                                                 | Fixed forward in the next rc, which restarts the clock                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Abort                  | Do not promote. `latest` stays on 2.3.x. Point `next` at the last good rc (or remove it) and `npm deprecate` the aborted rc. Fix forward.                                                              | Do not promote. `latest` stays on 2.4.x, patched from `release/2.4`. If the engine must leave master, the whole stack is reverted in one PR back to the 2.4.0 code; single engine PRs are never reverted alone, because each builds on the one before. The engine is then re-planned. `next` and the aborted rc are cleaned up as for 2.4.0.                             |

The rc issue names the consumers asked to run it, because silence from a client library mostly means nobody ran it. The `2.4-rc` and `3.0-rc` labels are created when the first rc of each release is cut.

The promotion commit is the last rc commit plus the version bump, with no merge in between, so `latest` ships the code the rc tested. `release.yml` takes `semver` and `tag` as independent inputs (`.github/workflows/release.yml:4-25`), so one forgotten field could publish an rc to `latest`; RGL-45 adds a guard step that fails when the version contains `-` and the tag is `latest`. After promotion there is no rollback, only fix-forward: a bad 2.4.0 or 3.0.0 is followed by a 2.4.1 or 3.0.1 that fixes or reverts, plus `npm deprecate` on the bad version. Moving the `latest` tag back is not relied on, which is why the rc window is the real gate.

### Maintained 2.x line

When the first 3.0 engine PR (RGL-23) merges, cut release/2.4 from the 2.4.0 tag. Propose 12 months of security and critical fixes after 3.0.0 ships, with the end date recorded in the 3.0 release notes. Publish the newest supported 2.4.x on the dedicated `2.x` dist-tag. It can share latest until 3.0 promotion; after that latest is 3.x and the 2.x tag remains on the maintained line. React 16.8/17 applications use `react-grid-layout@2.x`.

Patches land on release/2.4 and applicable fixes are cherry-picked to master. Master carries the engine and is never the source of a 2.4.x patch. Rehearse the release workflow from release/2.4 when cutting it, including the base branch and tag. The upstream candidate gate covers that line throughout its support window. Its 2.x consumer ledger and React smoke jobs stay active. A 3.0 abort keeps latest on a patched 2.4.x; it does not end 2.x support. OQ2 separately covers a 2.3.1 patch.

### Pixel checks

The per-frame bounding boxes are committed JSON (RGL-06), rounded to 0.01 px and recorded on the Linux CI image, so "byte-identical" for pixels means equal to that JSON. 2.4.0 runs them in Chromium only, because 2.4.0 does not change the pixel path: there is no engine, and RX-1 is already fixed on master (`1468c0f`), with this suite guarding it. A WebKit and a Firefox project each add a full e2e run per CI job, and Firefox has a known drop-path divergence to triage first (RX-11d). The engine release is where browser differences matter, so RGL-24's first commit adds both projects and records their JSON on the 2.4.x code, before the engine lands, and the engine commits keep it unchanged except the explicit §12 geometry changes, each recorded and reviewed separately. The wrapper-default DOM diff must preserve the positioned box unless a named BREAK changes its geometry. A frame is one sample after each awaited `page.mouse.move` settles, and only the recorder job writes the JSON (RGL-06).

### Errors in production

- A caught strategy error (§3 events) goes to `onError` when passed. Without it, the binding calls `console.error` once per error source per grid, in development and production alike, through `reportOnce` in `core/dev.ts`. Strategy errors are third-party bugs and rare, and a silent veto leaves an item that will not move with nothing in the console.
- `RGL_W005` (an invalid strategy result) uses the same production path.
- `createInitialState`, `syncProps`, `setConfig` and `setLayout` catch, report and keep the previous state. `createInitialState` keeps the validated input layout without compaction.
- Every other runtime warning is development only, through devWarn. JSDoc-only notices have codes for documentation but emit nothing at runtime.

### Upstream gating

RD and RR publish outside RGL's release train, and a caret range picks them up on any fresh install (`package.json:111-112`). So each upstream release follows this order:

1. RGL-05, RGL-07 and RGL-08 are merged and green.
2. The RD or RR release is published to the `next` tag.
3. A `workflow_dispatch` job in RGL, `.github/workflows/upstream-candidate.yml` (added in RGL-07), takes the package name and dist-tag. It checks out RGL master, runs `yarn add react-draggable@next` (or `react-resizable@next`), and runs the contract, interaction-golden and SSR suites, Playwright smoke and `live-interaction.spec.ts`. It does the same on `release/2.4` once that branch exists, which is the 2.4.x line. For an RD candidate, a third leg checks out the latest 2.3.x release tag, installs `react-draggable@next`, and runs that tag's own test suite.
4. Only when every applicable leg is green does the upstream latest tag move. After 3.0 promotion, master/3.x and the maintained release/2.4 leg both remain required; the RD 2.3.x leg remains while that caret range is supported. The RD and RR release PR templates carry this as a checklist line with a link to the run.

2.3.x is out of the RR gate only: it pins `react-resizable ^3.2.0` (`package.json:112`), so no RR 4.x candidate can reach it. It declares `react-draggable ^4.4.6` (`package.json:111`), so every RD 4.x release reaches it on a fresh install, and the floor-versus-latest cells cannot cover that, because they run master's code. That is why the RD gate has the third leg above.

RD-02 (the `offsetParent` cache) ships in its own RD release after 4.8.0, so a bad cache reverts without reverting the fixes.

### Golden diffs and changelog

- The golden suites fail on any changed or missing fixture in an ordinary run; only `GOLDEN_UPDATE=1` writes them.
- The PR template (`.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md`, RGL-45) gains four checklist lines: "golden diffs explained, each fixed bug's `knownBug` tag removed", "CHANGELOG entry for every expected diff", "`baseline.json`, the API report or the consumer fixtures changed: why (consumer-2.3 stays the immutable 2.x ledger; 3.0 diffs map to §12 and consumer-3.0)" and "new runtime APIs stay within ES2020". The last one holds because CI runs one Node version while consumers render on the server with older ones.
- Every job runs on the Node in `.nvmrc`. The global timsort patch (`test/util/setupTests.js:5-9`) changes tie order, so fixtures with `y: Infinity` or NaN, where comparators return NaN, are flagged in the corpus.
- There is no trailer-parsing or changelog script and no CODEOWNERS file. The repo has one maintainer, so CODEOWNERS adds no second reviewer, and a script could only check that a sentence exists, not that it is true. The tradeoff: a careless golden update is caught by review alone.
- The 2.4.0 notes open with Behavior changes and Deprecations. The 3.0.0 notes open with Breaking changes and link the guide/codemod and 2.x support window. Every §7 row with "Breaking if fixed" other than "No", and every §12 row, maps to one entry. The release PR checks the mapping row by row.

### Dev-warning codes (RGL-47)

Two helpers in `core/dev.ts`. `devWarn(scope, code, message)` warns once per scope (the grid's store or component instance) and code, checks `typeof process !== "undefined"` before reading `NODE_ENV`, and is a no-op in production. `reportOnce(scope, code, message)` is the same without the production guard, for `RGL_W005` and strategy errors.

| Code       | When                                                                                                                                                                                               | Release |
| ---------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------- |
| `RGL_W002` | A layout value or limit normalized at intake (§14). #2328 emits it as one uncoded development `console.warn` per clamping call; RGL-47 assigns the code                                            | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W003` | A pending layout changed geometry against the committed result, or a pending config changed `cols`, `maxRows` or the compactor key, when applied after an interaction (§3 engine config)           | 3.0.0   |
| `RGL_W005` | Invalid strategy result or ignored middleware edit field; step ignored (production too)                                                                                                            | 3.0.0   |
| `RGL_W006` | `dragConfig.threshold` and `dragConfig.activation` both set                                                                                                                                        | 3.0.0   |
| `RGL_W007` | Duplicate item ids at intake (C-B14); RGL-47 adds the check                                                                                                                                        | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W008` | Public factory/string-argument deprecation (JSDoc); runtime warning for getCompactor("wrap"). Private legacy mapping excluded                                                                      | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W009` | Direct GridItem caller explicitly passes live usePercentages/useCSSTransforms flags; message gives transform/absolute and percentage-style migrations (§12). Internal defaults and legacy excluded | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W010` | Legacy `verticalCompact={false}` on `ReactGridLayout` or `ResponsiveReactGridLayout`; the message names the component                                                                              | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W011` | `propose`, `stop` or `cancel` for an interaction that is not active; ignored (development only)                                                                                                    | 3.0.0   |
| `RGL_W012` | `onProposal` returned an edit at a drag or resize `start`; ignored, only `false` counts there (development only)                                                                                   | 3.0.0   |
| `RGL_W013` | validateLayout deprecation, JSDoc only                                                                                                                                                             | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W014` | Modern onDropDragOver; use native dropConfig.onDragOver                                                                                                                                            | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W015` | Modern data-grid; use explicit layout/layouts                                                                                                                                                      | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W016` | Five overlap-variant deprecations, JSDoc only                                                                                                                                                      | 2.4.0   |
| `RGL_W017` | Modern droppingItem; use dropConfig.defaultItem (item in 3.0)                                                                                                                                      | 2.4.0   |

§12 defines the replacements and warning policy.

Numbers 001 and 004 are unassigned: non-finite values are reported by `RGL_W002`, the one intake warning. The other codes are not renumbered.

The table lives in `docs/warnings.md`, and each message ends with its code.

**Release-number mapping.** Earlier panel notes called the engine release a second minor. In this revision the first release remains 2.4.0, and the engine release is 3.0.0. Later extension points are 3.x or a later major. Dependency floors and actionable deprecations are literal 2.4.0 work. The non-breaking compatibility rulings from earlier revisions apply to 2.4.x; §12 replaces them for 3.0.

---

## Alternatives Considered

### 1. Fix the bugs in place and keep three state machines

Rejected:

- Every bug in rgl-react §4 would need fixing in `GridLayout`, then again in `useGridLayout`, which has already drifted (RX-9).
- No extension point in §4 could ship once.
- The golden masters would still help, but the engine replay differential would not exist.

### 2. A generic plugin registry (`plugins={[...]}`)

Rejected:

- The pluggability survey found no demand that typed strategy slots cannot meet (pluggability §3).
- ProseMirror and TipTap show the cost: priority ordering, lifecycle hooks and cross-plugin state (pluggability §5).
- RGL's existing `LayoutConstraint[]` already gives ordered composition without a registry.

### 3. Engine as a class (gridstack's `GridStackEngine` model)

Rejected:

- A reducer over plain data is easier to replay, diff and freeze in tests.
- It maps onto `useSyncExternalStore` without an adapter.
- gridstack's lesson, separating the engine from the DOM, is kept (pluggability §5).

### 4. Replace react-draggable and react-resizable now

Rejected for this release train (§8):

- The savings are small: about 15 KB gzip, unminified.
- Fixing the known defects upstream is cheaper.
- An internal layer would give up 310 existing tests across the two repos.

### 5. Keep the engine release semver-minor

Rejected after Sam's revision 5 ruling. A minor would preserve the closed compactor identity, simulated drop callbacks, mutable shared defaults and exported internals just as the engine and binding change. §12 lists the concrete gain and migration for each accepted break. 2.4.0 still gets the tests, fixes and bounds to 2.x consumers first, and the maintained 2.x line gives them time to migrate. Root aliases, positional callbacks, CSS names, CJS and the in-package legacy entry stay because changing them buys no needed capability.
